is bi left-exact.

In order to ensure separability of the coproducts, in
3.22
.
4
we must restrict to countable families.

4
Stabilization

In this section we consider the Dwyer-Kan localization of the
\infty
-category
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
from (
3.3
) at the set of left-upper corner inclusions

A\to K\otimes A
for all
C^{*}
-algebras
A
, where
K
denotes the algebra of compact operators on a separable Hilbert space. It turns out that this localization is a left Bousfield localization generated by the tensor idempotent
K
. This fact makes is easy to understand the resulting category
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
. Its main new feature is semi-additivity.

We start with recalling the
\infty
-categorical background.
Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be an
\infty
-category with an endofunctor
L:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{C}}
and a natural transformation

\alpha:{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to L
. If for every object
C
the morphisms

\alpha_{L(C)},L(\alpha_{C}):L(C)\to L(L(C))

are equivalences, then
L:C\to L(C)
is the left-adjoint of a
left Bousfield localization
with unit
\alpha
(see
[
Lur09
, Prop. 5.2.7.4]
). It is also a Dwyer-Kan localization
at the set of morphisms
W_{L}:=\{\alpha_{C}\mid C\in{\mathbf{C}}\}
.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be left-exact
\infty
-category with a set of morphisms
W
.
We say that the Dwyer-Kan localization
L:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{C}}[W^{-1}]
is
left-exact
, if
{\mathbf{C}}[W^{-1}]
is left-exact and the functor
L
is left-exact. In this case, in addition to (
3.1
), we have an equivalence

L^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}({\mathbf{C}}[W^{-1}],{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex},W}({\mathbf{C}},{\mathbf{D}})

for any left-exact
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
.

In the present section we encounter a smashing left Bousfield localization which is generated by an
idempotent object
[
Lur
, Sec. 4.8.2]
.
An
idempotent object
in a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
with tensor unit
{\mathbf{1}}

is an
object
(\epsilon:{\mathbf{1}}\to A)
in the slice
{\mathbf{C}}_{{\mathbf{1}}/}
such that the map

\epsilon\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{A}:A\simeq{\mathbf{1}}\otimes A\to A\otimes A
is an equivalence. The inverse of this map is the multiplication of an essentially unique refinement of this object to a commutative algebra object in
{\mathbf{C}}
.

The functor
L_{A}:=A\otimes-:{\mathbf{C}}\to L_{A}{\mathbf{C}}
together with the
unit
{\mathtt{id}}\xrightarrow{\epsilon\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{-}}L_{A}
satisfies the conditions above ensuring that

L_{A}:{\mathbf{C}}\to L_{A}{\mathbf{C}}
is
the left-adjoint of a left Bousfield localization.
The localization

L_{A}:{\mathbf{C}}\to L_{A}{\mathbf{C}}
is also the Dwyer Kan localization inverting the set
W_{A}
of morphisms

B\xrightarrow{\epsilon\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{B}}A\otimes B
for all
B
. By associativity of the tensor product the set
W_{A}
is preserved by the functor
-\otimes C
for any object
C
of
{\mathbf{C}}
. It follows that the localization
L_{A}
has a symmetric monoidal refinement.

If
{\mathbf{C}}
has arbitrary coproducts, then by general properties of a left Bousfield localization so does
L_{A}{\mathbf{C}}
. Given a family
(B_{i})_{i\in I}
in
{\mathbf{C}}
we have

\coprod^{L_{A}{\mathbf{C}}}_{i\in I}L_{A}(B_{i})\simeq L_{A}(\coprod_{i\in I}^{{\mathbf{C}}}B_{i})\ .

Finally, if
{\mathbf{C}}
is left-exact and
\otimes
is bi-left exact, then
L_{A}
is a left-exact localization, and the induced tensor product

-\otimes-:L_{A}{\mathbf{C}}\times L_{A}{\mathbf{C}}\to L_{A}{\mathbf{C}}
is bi-left exact.

We will apply the above constructions to the left-exact symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
with one of the bi-left exact structures
\otimes_{\max}
or
\otimes_{\min}
and the tensor unit
{\mathbb{C}}
.

Recall that
K
is the algebra of compact operators on a separable Hilbert space
H
.
Let
e
be a minimal non-zero projection in
K
and
\epsilon:{\mathbb{C}}\to K
be the homomorphism
\lambda\mapsto\lambda e
.

Lemma 4.1
.

(\epsilon:{\mathbb{C}}\to K)
is an idempotent object in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
.

Proof.

For completeness of the presentation we add an argument for this well-known fact from
C^{*}
-algebra theory.
For
t
in
[0,1]
we define the isometry

U_{t}:L^{2}((-\infty,0])\to L^{2}((-\infty,1])
by

U_{t}(f)(x):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}f(x)&x\in(-\infty,-t)\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}f(\frac{x-t}{2})&x\in(-t,t)\\
0&x\in[t,1]\end{array}\right.\ .

Then
t\mapsto U_{t}
is strongly continuous and
U_{1}
is unitary.

We now construct a square

\textstyle{H\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{e\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{H}}
\scriptstyle{v}
\textstyle{H\otimes H\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{w}
\textstyle{L^{2}((-\infty,0])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{U_{0}}
\textstyle{L^{2}((-\infty,1])}

of isometric maps between Hilbert spaces. For

v
we choose any unitary isomorphism. In order to construct
w
we
choose an isomorphism
{\mathtt{im}}(e)\cong{\mathbb{C}}
and
a unitary isomorphism
v^{\prime}:{\mathtt{im}}(e)^{\perp}\otimes H\to L^{2}([0,1])
. Then we get the isomorhism

H\otimes H\cong{\mathtt{im}}(e)\otimes H\oplus{\mathtt{im}}(e)^{\perp}\otimes H\cong H\oplus{\mathtt{im}}(e)^{\perp}\otimes H
.
We then define
w_{|H}:=U_{0}\circ v
and
w_{|{\mathtt{im}}(e)^{\perp}\otimes H}:=v^{\prime}

Then
t\mapsto\phi_{t}:=w^{*}U_{t}v(-)v^{*}U_{t}^{*}w:K\to K\otimes K
is a point-norm continuous homotopy from

\epsilon\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{K}
to an isomorphism.
∎

For every
C^{*}
-algebra
A
the map

\kappa_{A}:A\simeq{\mathbb{C}}\otimes A\xrightarrow{\epsilon\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{A}}K\otimes A

(4.1)

is a
left upper corner inclusion
.

We let
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
denote the image of the functor
K\otimes-
.
Since we have an isomorphism
K\otimes K\cong K
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
, it consists precisely of the objects which are represented by
K
-stable
C^{*}
-algebras
, i.e. algebras
A
satisfying
A\cong K\otimes A
(note that this isomorphism is not related with the left upper corner inclusion).

Definition 4.2
.

We define the functor

L_{K}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{k}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\ ,\quad A\mapsto K\otimes A\ .

and the natural transformation

\kappa:{\mathtt{id}}\to L_{K}
with components
(\kappa_{A})_{A}
.

Note that
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
is locally small by
3.6
.

We define the functor

L_{h,K}:\>\>\>C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\xrightarrow{L_{h}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\xrightarrow{L_{K}}L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\ .

(4.2)

Corollary 4.3
.

1.

The functor
L_{K}
is the left-adjoint of a left Boufield localization

L_{K}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\leftrightarrows L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}:\mathrm{incl}

unit
\kappa
.

2.

The localization
L_{K}
is left-exact.

3.

L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
admits arbitrary small coproducts and
L_{K}
preserves them.

4.

For
\otimes_{?}
with
?\in\{\min,\max\}
the localization
L_{K}
has a symmetric monoidal refinement.

5.

The functor
-\otimes_{?}-:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\times L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
is bi-left exact.

For Assertion
3
we also used
3.9
.
The fact that
L_{K}
is a left Bousfield localization yields the following formula for the mapping spaces in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
. For
A,B
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
we have

{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,B)\simeq{\mathtt{Map}}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,K\otimes B)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{fijweiof24ffwrefwrefwff}}}{{\simeq}}\ell\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(A,K\otimes B)\ .

(4.3)

The pull-back along
L_{h,K}
induces for every

\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
an equivalence

L_{h,K}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{D}})\ ,

(4.4)

where the additional subscript indicates the full subcategory of
{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{D}})
of stable functors (see
2.4
.
2
).
For any symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category the pull-back along the symmetric monoidal refinement of
L_{h,K}

induces an equivalence

L_{h,K}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{h,s}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

(4.5)

For every left-exact
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
the pull-back along
L_{h,K}
induces an equivalence

L_{h,K}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s,Sch}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

(4.6)

If
{\mathbf{D}}
is in addition symmetric monoidal, then we have an equivalence

L_{h,K}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s,Sch}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

If
{\mathbf{C}}
is a pointed
\infty
-category with products and coproducts, then for any two objects
C
and
C^{\prime}
in
{\mathbf{C}}
we have a canonical morphism

(c\mapsto(c,0))\sqcup(c^{\prime}\mapsto(0,c^{\prime})):C\sqcup C^{\prime}\to C\times C^{\prime}\ .

A pointed
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
with products and coproducts
is called
semi-additive
if this canonical map is an equivalence for every
two objects
C
and
C^{\prime}
, see
[
Lur
, Def. 6.1.6.13]
.

If
{\mathbf{C}}
is semi-additive, then its mapping spaces
{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C,D)
have canonical refinements to commutative monoids in
\mathbf{Spc}
. In particular, every object of
{\mathbf{C}}
naturally becomes a commutative monoid and a commutative comonoid object in
{\mathbf{C}}

[
Lur
, Rem.6.1.6.14]
.

Note that
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
is pointed and admits products and coproducts by
4.3
.

Proposition 4.4
.

The
\infty
-category
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
is semi-additive.

Proof.

We consider two
C^{*}
-algebras
A
and
B
. We then have a canonical homomorphism

c:A*B\to A\oplus B

induced via the universal property of the free product
by the homomorphisms
A\to A\oplus B
,
a\mapsto(a,0)
and
B\to A\oplus B
,
b\mapsto(0,b)
.

Lemma 4.5

(
[
Cun87
, Prop. 3.1]
,
[
Mey00
, Prop. 5.3]
)
.

L_{h,K}(c):L_{h,K}(A*B)\to L_{h,K}(A\oplus B)
is an equivalence in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
.

A proof of
4.5
will be given below after the completion of the argument for
4.4
.
We consider
C^{*}
-algebras
A
and
B
. Then
the canonical map from the coproduct to the product of
L_{h,K}(A)
and
L_{h,K}(B)
in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
has the following factorization
over equivalences:

\displaystyle L_{h,K}(A)\sqcup L_{h,K}(B)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{wreogkpwegrewferf}.\ref{wetokgpwerferwfwref}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle L_{h,K}(A*B)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{weroigjowerfrefw}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle L_{h,K}(A\oplus B)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{weoijgowefgrefwrefrf}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle L_{K}(L_{h}(A)\times L_{h}(B))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{wreogkpwegrewferf}.\ref{wkogpwtgwerfwrefwr}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle L_{h,K}(A)\times L_{h,K}(B)

This finishes the proof of
4.4
assuming
4.5
.
∎

Proof of
4.5
.

Since we need some details of the proof of Lemma
4.5
later we recall the argument.
We first observe, using that
L_{h,K}\simeq L_{h,K}\circ{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(-)
,
that for any
C^{*}
-algebra
C
the left-upper corner inclusion
C\to{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(C)
is an equivalence in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
.

In the following we consider the
C^{*}
-algebras
A
and
B
as subsets of
A*B
.
We define a homomorphism

f:A\oplus B\xrightarrow{(a,b)\mapsto(a,b)}(A*B)\oplus(A*B)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{incl}}{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(A*B)\ ,\quad f(a,b):=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}a&0\\
0&b\end{array}\right)\ .

Then

f\circ c:A*B\to{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(A*B)
is determined by

a\mapsto\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}a&0\\
0&0\end{array}\right)\ ,\quad b\mapsto\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&0\\
0&b\end{array}\right)\ .

We consider the family of is unitaries

U_{t}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\cos(\frac{\pi t}{2})&-\sin(\frac{\pi t}{2})\\
\sin(\frac{\pi t}{2})&\cos(\frac{\pi t}{2})\end{array}\right)

(4.7)

in
{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(M(A*B))

and define
h_{t}:A*B\to{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(A*B)
by

h_{t}(a):=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}a&0\\
0&0\end{array}\right)\ ,\quad h_{t}(b)=U_{t}^{*}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&0\\
0&b\end{array}\right)U_{t}\ .

Then
h_{0}=f\circ c
and

h_{1}
is an upper corner inclusion.
We have

{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(c)\circ f:A\oplus B\to{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(A\oplus B)\ ,\quad(a,b)\mapsto\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}(a,0)&0\\
0&(0,b)\end{array}\right)\ .

We define the homotopy
h_{t}:A\oplus B\to{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(A\oplus B)
by

h_{t}(a,b):=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}(a,b\sin^{2}(\frac{\pi t}{2}))&(0,b\sin(\frac{\pi t}{2})\cos(\frac{\pi t}{2}))\\
(0,b\sin(\frac{\pi t}{2})\cos(\frac{\pi t}{2}))&(0,b\cos^{2}(\frac{\pi t}{2}))\end{array}\right)\ .

Then
h_{0}={\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(c)\circ f
and

h_{1}
is a upper corner inclusion.
∎

Remark 4.6
.

For two
C^{*}
-algebras
A
and
B
the mapping space
{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,B)
in the semi-additive
\infty
-category
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
is a commutative monoid in
\mathbf{Spc}
.
One is often interested
in its group completion. In this case the observation

10.13
might be helpful.

∎

4.5
can be generalized to countable coproducts and sums as follows.
Recall that

L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
admits small coproducts by
4.3
.
3
. Furthermore,
for a small family
(A_{i})_{i\in I}
of
C^{*}
-algebras we can also form the sum

\bigoplus_{i\in I}A_{i}:=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{F\subseteq I\ ,|F|<\infty}\bigoplus_{i\in F}A_{i}

(4.8)

which for infinite
I
should not be confused with the coproduct or product in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.
We still have a canonical map
c:*_{i\in I}A_{i}\to\bigoplus_{i\in I}A_{i}
.

Proposition 4.7
.

If
I
is countable, then
the canonical map induces an equivalence

L_{h,K}(c):L_{h,K}(*_{i\in I}A_{i})\to L_{h,K}(\bigoplus_{i\in I}A_{i})\ .

Proof.

For finite
I
this follows by a finite induction from
4.5
.
We now assume that

I={\rm Nature}
.
We define the map

f:\bigoplus_{i\in I}A_{i}\to K\otimes*_{i\in I}A_{i}\ ,\quad f((a_{i})_{i}):={\mathtt{diag}}(a_{0},a_{1},a_{2},\dots)\ .

Note that
\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{i\to\infty}\|a_{i}\|=0
so that
this diagonal matrix really belongs to
K\otimes*_{i\in I}A_{i}
.

The composition
f\circ c:*_{i\in I}A_{i}\to K\otimes*_{i\in I}A_{i}
is determined by

a_{i}\mapsto{\mathtt{diag}}(0,\dots 0,a_{i},0\dots)
with
a_{i}
at the
i
th place.
We define
a homotopy
h_{t}:*_{i\in I}A_{i}\to K\otimes*_{i\in I}A_{i}
such that for

t\in[1-\frac{1}{i+1},1-\frac{1}{i+2}]
it rotates in the coordinates
0
and
i
as in the proof of
4.5
.
Then
h_{t}
is continuous as a map
[0,1]\to\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(*_{i\in I}A_{i},K\otimes*_{i\in I}A_{i})
. Indeed,

h_{t}
is continuous on the subalgebras
*_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}
for all
n
in
{\rm Nature}
. Since
their union is dense and

h_{t}
is uniformly bounded we conclude continuity.
We have

h_{0}=f\circ c
and

h_{1}
is a left upper corner embedding. This implies that

L_{h,K}(f)\circ L_{h,K}(c)\simeq L_{h,K}({\mathtt{id}}_{*_{i\in I}A_{i}})
.

The composition

({\mathtt{id}}_{K}\otimes c)\circ f:\bigoplus_{i\in I}A_{i}\to K\otimes\bigoplus_{i\in I}A_{i}
is determined by

(a_{i})_{i}\mapsto{\mathtt{diag}}((a_{0},0,\dots),(0,a_{1},0,\dots),(0,0,a_{2},\dots),\dots)\ .

We
define the homotopy
l_{t}:\bigoplus_{i\in I}A_{i}\to K\otimes\bigoplus_{i\in I}A_{i}

such that
for
t\in[1-\frac{1}{i+1},1-\frac{1}{i+2}]
it rotates in the coordinates
(0,i)
and
(i,i)
as in the proof of
4.5
.
Then

l_{t}
is continuous as a map
[0,1]\to\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(\bigoplus_{i\in I}A_{i},L_{K}(\bigoplus_{i\in I}A_{i})
.
Indeed,

l_{t}
is continuous on the subalgebras
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}
for all
n
in
{\rm Nature}
. Since their union
is dense and

l_{t}
is uniformly bounded we can conclude continuity.
We have

l_{0}=({\mathtt{id}}_{K}\otimes c)\circ f

and

l_{1}
is a left upper corner embedding.
This implies that

L_{h,K}(c)\circ L_{h,K}(f)\simeq L_{h,K}({\mathtt{id}}_{\bigoplus_{i\in I}A_{i}})
.
∎

Example 4.8
.

For any
C^{*}
-algebra
A
we let
\mathrm{Proj}(A)
denote the topological space of projections in
A
. The functor
\mathrm{Proj}(-):C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to{\mathbf{Top}}
is corepresented by
the
C^{*}
-algebra
{\mathbb{C}}
. Indeed, for a
C^{*}
-algebra
A
we have a homeomorphism

\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}({\mathbb{C}},A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Proj}(A)\ ,\quad f\mapsto f(1)\ .

We define the topological space of stable projections in
A
by

\mathrm{Proj}^{s}(A):=\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}({\mathbb{C}},K\otimes A)

which becomes an
H
-space with respect to the block sum operations.
Using the semi-additivity of
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
its underlying space

\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(A):=\ell\mathrm{Proj}^{s}(A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{vwihviuewhviwevewre}}}{{\simeq}}{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}({\mathbb{C}},A)

has a refinement to an object of
\mathbf{CMon}(\mathbf{Spc})
, i.e., a commutative monoid object in spaces. It will be called the monoid of
stable projections
in
A
.

We will see in
10.8
, that for unital
A
the group completion of the
commutative monoid
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(A)
is equivalent to the
K
-theory space of
A
.

∎

Example 4.9
.

For any unital
C^{*}
-algebra
A
we let
U(A)
denote the topological group of unitaries in
A
.
The functor
U:C^{\ast}\mathbf{Alg}\to\mathbf{Groups}({\mathbf{Top}})
is corepresented by
the
C^{*}
-algebra
C(S^{1})
. Let
u:S^{1}\to{\mathbb{C}}
be the inclusion considered as an element of

U(C(S^{1}))
. Then we have an isomorphism

\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}_{u}}(C(S^{1}),A)\xrightarrow{\cong}U(A)\ ,\quad f\mapsto f(u)

of topological groups,
where the subscript
u
indicates that we consider the subspace of
\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(C(S^{1}),A)
of unit-preserving homomorphisms.

Using the unitalization functor
(-)^{u}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to C^{\ast}\mathbf{Alg}
we define the functor

U^{s}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{Groups}({\mathbf{Top}})\ ,\quad A\mapsto\{U\in U((K\otimes A)^{u})\mid U-1_{(K\otimes A)^{u}}\in K\otimes A\}

(4.9)

which associates to
A
the topological group of
stable unitaries
.
The stable unitaries functor is corepresented by the suspension
S({\mathbb{C}})
of
{\mathbb{C}}
. Indeed,
using the split-exact sequence

0\to S({\mathbb{C}})\xrightarrow{i}C(S^{1})\xrightarrow{{\mathtt{ev}}_{1}}{\mathbb{C}}\to 0

(4.10)

we identify
C(S^{1})
with the unitalization of
S({\mathbb{C}})
.
Then the unitalization functor induces an identification

\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(S({\mathbb{C}}),K\otimes A)\cong U^{s}(A)\ .

(4.11)

By (
4.3
) we have an equivalence of spaces

{\mathcal{U}}^{s}(A):={\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(S({\mathbb{C}}),A)\simeq\ell U^{s}(A)

(4.12)

which equips the commutative monoid on the left with a second group structure.
These two structures distribute and therefore coincide by an Eckmann-Hilton type argument.
It follows that the commutative monoid
{\mathcal{U}}^{s}(A)
is already an object of
\mathbf{CGroups}(\mathbf{Spc})
, i.e., a commutative group object in spaces. It will be called the
group of stable unitaries
.

Unfolding definitions we see that the monoid structure on
{\mathcal{U}}^{s}(A)
comes from the block-sum of unitaries in
(K\otimes A)^{u}
while the other group structures is given by the multiplication of unitaries.
In
9.4
we will show that
{\mathcal{U}}^{s}(A)
is equivalent to a one-fold delooping of the
K
-theory space of
A
.

∎

Example 4.10
.

We consider two
C^{*}
-algebras
A
and
B
and homomorphisms
f,f^{\prime}:A\to B
.
We say that
{\mathtt{im}}(f)\perp{\mathtt{im}}(f^{\prime})
if
f(a)f^{\prime}(a^{\prime})=0=f^{\prime}(a^{\prime})f(a)
for all
a,a^{\prime}
in
A
.
In this case we can define a homomorphism

f+f^{\prime}:A\to B\ ,\quad a\mapsto f(a)+f(a^{\prime})\ .

We then have an equivalence

L_{h,K}(f)+L_{h,K}(f^{\prime})\simeq L_{h,K}(f+f^{\prime})

in
{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,B)
, where the sum on the left is the monoid structure on the mapping space coming from the semi-additivity of
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
(see
6.1
).
Indeed, this sum is represented by

A\xrightarrow{{\mathtt{diag}}(f,f^{\prime})}{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(B)\to K\otimes B\ .

There is a rotation homoptopy
\phi_{t}
from

{\mathtt{diag}}(0,f^{\prime})
to
{\mathtt{diag}}(f^{\prime},0)
such that
{\mathtt{im}}(\phi_{t})\perp{\mathtt{im}}({\mathtt{diag}}(f,0))
for all
t
. Hence

{\mathtt{diag}}(f,0)+\phi_{t}
is a homotopy from
{\mathtt{diag}}(f,f^{\prime})
to
{\mathtt{diag}}(f+f^{\prime},0)
. This implies the assertion.

∎

Since
K
is separable the functor
L_{K}
restricts to

L_{\mathrm{sep},K}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}

with an essentially small target.
Together with
3.22
,
4.3
and
4.4
this implies the following statements:

Corollary 4.11
.

1.

We have a commutative square

\textstyle{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{L_{\mathrm{sep},K}}
\textstyle{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{L_{K}}
\textstyle{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}

of
\infty
-categories where the vertical arrows are left Bousfield localizations and the horizontal arrows are fully faithful.

2.

The square in
1
has a refinement to a diagram of symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories and symmetric monoidal functors for
\otimes_{?}
with
?\in\{\min,\max\}
such that
L_{\mathrm{sep},K}
becomes a symmetric monoidal localization.

3.

The localization
L_{\mathrm{sep},K}
is left-exact.

4.

L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
admits countable coproducts and
L_{\mathrm{sep},K}
preserves them.

5.

The functor
-\otimes_{?}-:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\times L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
is bi-left exact.

6.

L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
is semi-additive.

The pull-back along
L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K}:=L_{\mathrm{sep},K}\circ L_{\mathrm{sep},h}

induces for every

\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
an equivalence

L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

For any symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category the pull-back along the symmetric monoidal refinement of
L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K}

induces an equivalence

L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{h,s}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

For every left-exact
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
the pull-back along
L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K}
induces an equivalence

L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s,Sch}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

If
{\mathbf{D}}
is in addition symmetric monoidal, then we have the equivalence

L^{*}_{\mathrm{sep},h,K}:{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{\mathrm{lex}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{h,s,Sch}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

5
Forcing exactness

In this subsection we describe left-exact Dwyer-Kan localizations

L_{!}:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}

for
!
in
\{\mathrm{splt},\mathrm{se},\mathrm{ex}\}

designed such that the composition

L_{h,K,!}:=L_{!}\circ L_{h,K}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}

(see (
4.2
) for
L_{h,K}
)
sends exact (for
!=\mathrm{ex}
) or semi-split exact (for
!=\mathrm{se}
) or split exact (for
!=\mathrm{splt}
) sequences of
C^{*}
-algebras to fibre sequences.
We further analyse the compatibility of
L_{!}
with the symmetric monoidal structures.

Given an exact exact sequence
0\to A\to B\xrightarrow{f}C\to 0
of
C^{*}
-algebras, using the mapping cylinder construction described in
3.16

we can produce
a diagram of exact sequences of
C^{*}
-algebras

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{f}}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{h_{f}}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C(f)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{f}}
\textstyle{Z(f)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{f}}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\hat{s}}
\textstyle{0}

(5.1)

We write elements of the mapping cone
C(f)
of
f
as pairs
(b,\gamma)
with
b
in
B
and
\gamma
in
PC
(see (
3.6
)) such that
f(b)=\gamma(0)
and
\gamma(1)=0
and consider
A
as a subset of
B
. With this notation the map
\iota_{f}
is given by
\iota_{f}(a):=(a,0)
. This map is the inclusion of an ideal and the
C^{*}
-algebra

Q(f)
is defined as the quotient. We have an isomorphism

Q(f)\cong\{(c,\gamma)\in C\times PC\mid\gamma(0)=c\ ,\gamma(1)=0\}\cong C_{0}([0,1))\otimes C

which implies that

Q
is contractible.
If
f
admits a cpc split
s
(or a split), then we get induced cpc splits (or splits)

\tilde{s}(c):=(s(c),{\mathtt{const}}_{c})

and
\hat{s}(c,\gamma)=(s(c),\gamma)
as indicated.

We consider
a functor
F:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to{\mathbf{C}}
to a pointed
\infty
-category.
Versions of the following observation were basic to the approach in
[
Hig90a
]
,
[
CS86
]
.

Proposition 5.1
.

We assume that
F
is homotopy invariant and
reduced,
and that it sends mapping cone sequences to fibre sequences.
Then the following statements are equivalent:

1.

F
is exact (semiexact, or split-exact, respectively).

2.

For every exact (sem-split exact, or split exact, respectively) sequence of
C^{*}
-algebras

0\to I\xrightarrow{i}A\xrightarrow{\pi}Q\to 0
with
Q
contractible the map
F(i)
is an equivalence.

Proof.

We assume that

F
satisfies
1
.
Then
F
sends the exact (semi-split exact, or split exact) sequence
0\to I\xrightarrow{i}A\xrightarrow{\pi}Q\to 0
to a fibre sequence

F(I)\to F(A)\to F(Q)

with
F(Q)\simeq 0
.
Consequently,

F(i)
is an equivalence.

Conversely we assume that
F
satisfies
2
.
We
consider a general exact (semi-split exact or split exact) sequence
0\to A\to B\xrightarrow{f}C\to 0
and form the
diagram (
5.1
). By assumption

F
sends the lower horizontal sequence to a fibre sequence and

F(\iota_{f})
is an equivalence.
By homotopy invariance of
F
also
F(h_{f})
is an equivalence.
We can therefore conclude that

F(A)\to F(B)\to F(C)
is fibre sequence, too.
∎

Recall the
3.14
of Schochet exactness of a functor in terms of Schochet fibrant squares.
By our experience, Schochet exactness is either obvious from the construction or very difficult to verify. The following result shows that for stable target categories homotopy invariance and semiexactness together imply Schochet exactness. We consider homotopy invariance and semiexactnes as properties which are much closer
to the usual
C^{*}
-algebraic business.

Lemma 5.2
.

A homotopy invariant and semiexact functor from
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
to a stable
\infty
-category is Schochet exact.

Proof.

Let
F:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to{\mathbf{D}}
be a homotopy invariant and semiexact functor to a stable
\infty
-category.
Then
F
is reduced. Since
{\mathbf{D}}
is stable, in order to show Schochet exactness by
3.15
it suffices
to show
that
F
sends any Schochet exact sequence
0\to A\to B\to C\to 0
to a fibre sequence.

We apply
F
to the diagram (
5.1
) and get a diagram

The middle horizontal line is a fibre sequence since
F
is semiexact and the middle horizontal sequence in (
5.1
) is semi-split exact.
The functor
L_{h}
sends both horizontal sequences in (
5.1
) to fibre sequences since they are Schochet exact.
Since
h_{f}
is a homotopy equivalence we see that
L_{h}(h_{f})
and hence also
L_{h}(\iota_{f})
are equivalences. Since
F
is homotopy invariant it factorizes over
L_{h}
and therefore
F(\iota_{f})
is also an equivalence. We can now conclude that the upper horizontal sequence in the diagram above is a fibre sequence.
∎

We consider exact sequences

0\to I\xrightarrow{i}A\xrightarrow{\pi}Q\to 0

and define the following sets of morphisms
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
:

\displaystyle\hat{W}_{\mathrm{splt}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\{i\mid\mbox{for all split-exact sequences with $Q$ contractible}\}

(5.2)

\displaystyle\hat{W}_{\mathrm{se}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\{i\mid\mbox{for all semi-split exact sequences with $Q$ contractible}\}

\displaystyle\hat{W}_{\mathrm{ex}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\{i\mid\mbox{for all exact sequences with $Q$ contractible}\}

We will denote the closures in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
under equivalences of their images by
L_{h,K}
by the same symbols.

Remark 5.3
.

A natural idea would be to form the Dwyer-Kan localizations of
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
at the sets

\hat{W}_{!}
defined above. But there is no reason that these localizations are left-exact. In order to produce left-exact
localizations we must localize at the closures of the above sets under pull-backs and the
2
-out-of-
3
property.

∎

In the following a set
W
of morphisms in an
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
is always assumed to be closed under equivalences.
The set
W

is
closed under pull-backs
if for every cartesian square

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C}

in
{\mathbf{C}}
with
f
in
W
also
g
belongs to
W
.

The set
W
has the
2
-out-of
3
-property
if the fact that two out of
f,g,g\circ f

belong to
W
implies that the third also belongs to
W
.

Example 5.4
.

If
F:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{D}}
is a functor between
\infty
-categories, then the set
W
of morphisms in
{\mathbf{C}}
which are
sent by
F
to equivalences has the
2
-out-of
3
-property. If
F
is a left-exact functor between left-exact
\infty
-categories, then
W
is also closed under pull-backs.
∎

Let

{\mathbf{C}}
be a left-exact
\infty
-category with a set of morphisms
W
.

Lemma 5.5
.

If
W
has the
2
-out-of
3
-property and is closed under pull-backs, then the Dwyer-Kan localization
L:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{C}}[W^{-1}]
is left-exact.

Proof.

The triple
({\mathbf{C}},W,{\mathbf{C}})
is a category of fibrant objects in the sense of
[
Cis19
, Def. 7.4.12 and Def. 7.5.7]
. The Assertion
1
therefore follows from
[
Cis19
, Prop. 7.5.6]

∎

Let

{\mathbf{C}}
be a left-exact
\infty
-category with a set of morphisms
\hat{W}
, and let
W
be the minimal
subset of morphisms containing
\hat{W}
which has the
2
-out-of
3
-property and is closed under pull-backs. Then for every left-exact
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
the canonical inclusion

{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex},W}({\mathbf{C}},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex},\hat{W}}({\mathbf{C}},{\mathbf{D}})

(5.3)

is an equivalence.

For
!
in
\{\mathrm{ex},\mathrm{se},\mathrm{splt}\}
we define

W_{!}
as the smallest set of morphisms in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
which is closed under pull-backs and has the
2
-out-of
3
-property, and which contains
\hat{W}_{!}
from (
5.2
).

Definition 5.6
.

We define the Dwyer-Kan localization

L_{!}:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}

at the set
W_{!}
.

Note that by construction
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}
is a large
\infty
-category. As we localize at a large set of morphisms we lose the property of being locally small at this point.

We define the functor

L_{h,K,!}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\xrightarrow{L_{h}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\xrightarrow{L_{K}}L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\xrightarrow{L_{!}}L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}\ .

We let
{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s,!+Sch}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{D}})
denote the full subcategory of homotopy invariant and stable functors which are Schochet exact and in addition
exact for
!=\mathrm{ex}
(semiexact for
!=\mathrm{se}
or split exact for
!=\mathrm{splt}
, respectively).

Proposition 5.7
.

1.

The localization
L_{!}
is left-exact.

2.

Pull-back along
L_{h,K,!}
induces for every left-exact
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
an equivalence

L_{h,K,!}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s,!+Sch}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

(5.4)

3.

L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}
is semi-additive and
L_{!}
preserves coproducts.

Proof.

The Assertion
1
follows from
5.5
.

We next show Assertion
2
.
For any
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
we have restriction functors

{\mathbf{Fun}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L_{!}^{*}}}{{\simeq}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{W_{!}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L_{h,K}^{*}}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{D}})\ ,

where the first is an equivalence by the universal property of the Dwyer-Kan localization
L_{!}
.
The second functor is
the restriction of the equivalence (
4.4
) and hence fully faithful.
If
{\mathbf{D}}
is left-exact, then by
1

the first functor restricts to the first equivalence in

{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L_{!}^{*}}}{{\simeq}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex},W_{!}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{erfwerferfwef}}}{{\simeq}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex},\hat{W}_{!}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

(5.5)

Finally, by
5.1
the equivalence (
4.6
) restricts to an equivalence

{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex},\hat{W}_{!},}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L_{h,K}^{*}}}{{\simeq}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s,!+Sch}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

The composition of these equivalences gives (
5.4
).

Assertion
3
is a general fact about left-exact localizations of left-exact
\infty
-categories which are in addition semi-additive.
∎

Next we consider the symmetric monoidal structures. We allow the following combinations of
!
and
?
:

!
\backslash
?

min

max

splt

yes

yes

se

yes

yes

ex

no

yes

The combination
(\mathrm{ex},\min)
is excluded since the minimal tensor product does not preserve exact sequences.

Proposition 5.8
.

1.

The localization
L_{!}
has a symmetric monoidal refinement.

2.

The functor
-\otimes_{?}-:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}\times L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}
is bi-left exact.

3.

Pull-back along
L_{h,K,!}
induces for every left-exact
\infty
-category an equivalence

L_{h,K,!}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{\mathrm{lex}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s,!+Sch}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{D}})

(5.6)

for any symmetric monoidal and left-exact
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
.

Proof.

We first observe that the functor

A\otimes_{?}-:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}

preserves the set
\hat{W}_{!}
defined in (
5.2
).
Indeed if
!=\mathrm{ex}
and
?=\max
, then we use that this functor preserves exact sequences and
contractible objects. It is at this point where we must exclude the combination
!=\mathrm{ex}
and
?=\min
.

If
!
is in
\{\mathrm{se},\mathrm{splt}\}
, then this functor preserves semi-split exact or split exact sequences and
contractible objects for both
?=\min
and
?=\max
.

Let
\tilde{W}_{!}
be set of morphisms in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
which are sent to equivalences by
L_{!}
.
Then for every left-exact
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
we have an equivalence

{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex},\hat{W}_{!}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{erfwerferfwef}}}{{\simeq}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex},W_{!}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h},{\mathbf{D}})\simeq{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex},\tilde{W}_{!}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

(5.7)

By
4.3
.
5
and
5.7
.
1
the composition

L_{!}\circ(A\otimes_{?}-):L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}

(5.8)

is left-exact. By the discussion above it inverts
\hat{W}_{!}
. It then follows from (
5.7
) that
A\otimes_{?}-
preserves the set
\tilde{W}_{!}
. Since
L_{!}
is also the Dwyer-Kan localization of
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
at
\tilde{W}_{!}

we conclude that the localization
L_{!}
has a symmetric monoidal refinement, hence Assertion
1
.

For Assertion
2
we note that
the induced functor

A\otimes_{?}-:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}

is left exact since
it is the preimage of (
5.8
) under the equivalence

L_{!}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!},L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex},W_{!}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h},L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!})

(see
5.8
.
1
).

We finally show Assertion
3
.
Assertion
1
implies the first equivalence in

{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L_{!}^{*}}}{{\simeq}}{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{W_{!}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h},{\mathbf{D}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{D}})

whose second arrow is a restriction of the equivalence (
4.5
) and hence fully faithful.
We now restrict the domain to (lax) symmetric monoidal functors which are in addition left-exact and get

{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{\mathrm{lex}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L_{!}^{*}}}{{\simeq}}{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{W_{!},\mathrm{lex}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s,!+Sch}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

The second arrow indeed takes values in the indicated subcategory by
5.7
.
2
.
In order to see that it is essential surjective consider a functor
F
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s,!+Sch}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{D}})
.
Then by (
4.5
) there is an essentially unique (lax) symmetric monoidal functor
\tilde{F}:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\to{\mathbf{D}}
such that
L_{h,K}^{*}\tilde{F}\simeq F
. By
5.7
.
2
the functor
\tilde{F}

is left-exact and inverts
W_{!}
. Thus
\tilde{F}
belongs to
{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{W_{!},\mathrm{lex}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h},{\mathbf{D}})
.
∎

The constructions above have versions for the category of separable
C^{*}
-algebras. We let
\hat{W}_{\mathrm{sep},!}
denote the analog of
\hat{W}_{!}
from (
5.2
) for separable algebras and
W_{\mathrm{sep},!}
be the smallest subset of morphisms containing
\hat{W}_{\mathrm{sep},!}
which has the
2
-out-of-
3
-property and is closed under pull-backs.
For
!
in
\{\mathrm{ex},\mathrm{se},\mathrm{splt}\}
we define the Dwyer-Kan localization

L_{\mathrm{sep},!}:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}

(5.9)

at
W_{\mathrm{sep},!}
.
Since
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
is locally small and essentially small,
the set of equivalence classes in
W_{\mathrm{sep},!}
is small. This implies that
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}
is still
essentially small and locally small.

We define the composition

L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,!}:=L_{\mathrm{sep},!}\circ L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}\ .

(5.10)

Then we have the following statements.

Proposition 5.9
.

1.

The localization
L_{\mathrm{sep},!}
is left-exact.

2.

L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}
is semi-additive and
L_{\mathrm{sep},!}
preserves finite coproducts.

3.

Pull-back along
L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,!}
induces for every left-exact
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
an equivalence

L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,!}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s,!+Sch}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

(5.11)

4.

The localization
L_{\mathrm{sep},!}
has a symmetric monoidal refinement.

5.

We have a commutative square of symmetric monoidal functors

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 36.86382pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-34.59991pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 61.15955pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.86382pt\raise-31.38889pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern (5.12)

6.

The functor
-\otimes_{?}-:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}\times L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}
is bi-left exact.

7.

Pull-back along
L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,!}
induces for every left-exact
\infty
-category an equivalence

L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,!}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{\mathrm{lex}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s,!+Sch}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{D}})

(5.13)

for any symmetric monoidal and left-exact
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
.

Remark 5.10
.

In contrast to
4.3
.
3

we do no not know whether
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}
admits infinite coproducts.

In contrast to the upper horizontal arrow in (
5.12
) the lower horizontal arrow in this square is not known to be fully faithful, see
3.21
.

∎

Remark 5.11
.

If the target category
{\mathbf{D}}
is stable and we consider
!
in
\{\mathrm{se},\mathrm{ex}\}
, then by
5.2
(or its separable version) we could remove the superscripts
+Sch
on the right-rand sides of (
5.4
), (
5.6
), (
5.11
), and (
5.13
).
∎

6
Bott periodicity

In this section we analyse the Toeplitz extension

0\to K\to{\mathcal{T}}\to C(S^{1})\to 0

from the homotopy theoretic point of view. The section is essentially an
\infty
-categorical version of
[
Cun84
, Sec. 4]
. The main result is
6.11
.

We start with recalling some generalities on group objects in
\infty
-categories.
If
{\mathbf{C}}
is an
\infty
-category admitting cartesian products, then we can consider the
\infty
-category of
commutative algebras in
{\mathbf{C}}
for the cartesian monoidal structure which will be called the
\infty
-category
of
commutative monoids

\mathbf{CMon}({\mathbf{C}})
. Let
C
be a commutative monoid with multiplication map
m:C\times C\to C
. It is called a
commutative group
if
the shear map

C\times C\xrightarrow{(c,c^{\prime})\mapsto(c,m(c,c^{\prime}))}C\times C

is an equivalence.
We let
\mathbf{CGroups}({\mathbf{C}})
denote the full subcategory of
\mathbf{CMon}({\mathbf{C}})
of commutative groups.
Note that we have used these notions already for
{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Spc}
.

Dually, if
{\mathbf{C}}
admits coproducts, then we can consider the
\infty
-categories of

cocommutative comonoids

\mathbf{coCMon}({\mathbf{C}})
and its full subcategory
cocommutative cogrouops

\mathbf{coCGroup}({\mathbf{C}})
.

Example 6.1
.

In a semi-additive
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
every object is naturally a commutative monoid and a commutative comonoid. The
functors forgetting the commutative monoid or comonoid structures are equivalences:

\mathbf{CMon}({\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{C}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\leftarrow}}\mathbf{coCMon}({\mathbf{C}})\ .

Let
C
be in an object of
{\mathbf{C}}
. Then the multiplication and comultiplication maps of the corresponding monoid or comonoid are given by

C\times C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\leftarrow}}C\sqcup C\xrightarrow{\mathrm{codiag}}C\ ,\quad C\xrightarrow{{\mathtt{diag}}}C\times C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\leftarrow}}C\sqcup C\ .

Moreover, the conditions of being a group or a cogroup are equivalent.

For any two objects
C,C^{\prime}
in
{\mathbf{C}}
the mapping space

{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C,C^{\prime})
has a natural refinement to an object of
\mathbf{CMon}(\mathbf{Spc})
.
The object
C^{\prime}
is a group if and only if
{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C,C^{\prime})
is a group for all objects
C
.

∎

Example 6.2
.

The last assertion in (
6.1
) reduces the verification of the group property for an object in a semi-additive category to
the case of monoids in spaces. In this case we have a simple criterion.
An object
X
in
\mathbf{CMon}(\mathbf{Spc})
is a group if an only if the monoid
\pi_{0}X
is a group.
∎

Lemma 6.3
.

If
{\mathbf{C}}
is semi-additive and left-exact, then
\Omega:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{C}}
(see
3.19
) takes values in commutative groups.

Proof.

Let
C^{\prime}
be an object of
{\mathbf{C}}
. We must show that
\Omega C^{\prime}
is a group. To this end we will show that
{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C,\Omega C^{\prime})\simeq\Omega{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C,C^{\prime})
is a group in
\mathbf{Spc}
for any object
C
of
{\mathbf{C}}
.
We now use
6.2
in order to reduce the problem to the set of components.

Note that
\pi_{0}\Omega{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C,C^{\prime})\cong\pi_{1}{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C,C^{\prime})
clearly has a group structure
\sharp
as a fundamental group. This structure distributes over the commutative monoid structure
+

on
\pi_{0}{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C,\Omega C^{\prime})
coming from the semi-additivity in the sense that

(a+b)\sharp(c+d)=(a\sharp c)+(b\sharp d)
. The Eckmann-Hilton argument implies that both structures coincide. In particular, the commutative monoid structure
+
is a commutative group structure.
∎

To every unital
C^{*}
-algebra
A
we functorially associate the topological space

I(A):=\{v\in A\mid v^{*}v=1_{A}

of isometries in
A
.
By definition, the Toeplitz algebra
{\mathcal{T}}
is the isometries classifier in
C^{\ast}\mathbf{Alg}
. It
contains a universal isometry
v
such that

\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}_{u}({\mathcal{T}},A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}I(A)\ ,\quad f\mapsto f(v)

is a homeomorphism for every unital
C^{*}
-algebra
A
.

Recall from
4.9
that
C(S^{1})
is the unitaries classifier in
C^{\ast}\mathbf{Alg}
with the universal unitary
u
.
Since unitaries are in particular isometries we have a canonical unital homomorphism

\pi:{\mathcal{T}}\to C(S^{1})\ ,\quad\pi(v)=u\ .

Since
C(S^{1})
is generated by
u
, the homomorphism
\pi

is surjective. We let
K
denote the kernel of
\pi
. We thus have
the
Toeplitz exact sequence

0\to K\to{\mathcal{T}}\xrightarrow{\pi}C(S^{1})\to 0\ .

(6.1)

It is known that
{\mathcal{T}}
is separable and nuclear.
The projection
e:=1_{{\mathcal{T}}}-vv^{*}
belongs to
K
, and
the algebra
K
is generated by the family of minimal pairwise orthogonal projections
(v^{n}ev^{*,n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
. This provides an identification of
K
with the algebra of compact operators on a separable Hilbert space and justifies the notation. Note that
e
is
a minimal projection in
K
.

Using the universal property of
{\mathcal{T}}
and the unit we define homomorphisms

q:{\mathcal{T}}\to{\mathbb{C}}\ ,\quad v\mapsto 1\ ,\qquad j:{\mathbb{C}}\to{\mathcal{T}}\ ,\quad 1\mapsto 1_{{\mathcal{T}}}\ .

We consider a functor

F:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to{\mathbf{C}}
to a semi-additive
\infty
-category.

Proposition 6.4
.

If
F
is homotopy invariant, stable, split-exact and takes values in
group objects,
then
F(j)
and
F(q)
are mutually inverse equivalences.

Proof.

The equality

j\circ q={\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}

implies that
F(j)\circ F(q)\simeq{\mathtt{id}}_{F({\mathbb{C}})}
. It remains to show that

F(q)\circ F(j)\simeq{\mathtt{id}}_{F({\mathcal{T}})}
. To this end we construct the following diagram of
C^{*}
-algebras:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 138.15277pt\raise-63.31998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 138.15277pt\raise-63.31998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern
