212.20969pt\raise-63.31998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 212.20969pt\raise-63.31998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

The lower horizontal sequence is the tensor product of the Toeplitz sequence with
{\mathcal{T}}
.
The algebra
\bar{\mathcal{T}}
is defined such that the right square is a pull-back. This determines the homomorphisms
r
and
p
.
The maps
\kappa
,
\tau
and
\alpha
are determined by the universal property of
{\mathcal{T}}
and the relations

\kappa:v\mapsto e\otimes v\ ,\quad\alpha:v\mapsto v(1-e)\otimes 1_{{\mathcal{T}}}\ ,\quad\tau:v\mapsto u\otimes 1_{{\mathcal{T}}}\ .

Since
(\pi\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\circ\alpha=\tau
we can define the map
s
by the universal property of the pull-back
such that
r\circ s=\alpha
and
p\circ s={\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
.
The last equality implies that the upper horizontal sequence is split-exact.
By the split-exactness of
F
we get an equivalence

F(\iota)\oplus F(s):F(K\otimes{\mathcal{T}})\oplus F({\mathcal{T}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}F(\bar{\mathcal{T}})\ .

In particular we can conclude that
F(\iota)
is monomorphism.

Since

{\mathtt{im}}(r\iota\kappa)\perp{\mathtt{im}}(\alpha)
(see
4.10
) we can define
following homomorphisms

\phi_{0}:=\alpha+r\iota\kappa{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\ ,\quad\phi_{1}:=\alpha+r\iota\kappa(j\circ q)

(6.2)

from
{\mathcal{T}}
to
{\mathcal{T}}\otimes{\mathcal{T}}
.
It has been shown in
[
Cun84
, Sec. 4]
(see
[
Fri
]
for a nice presentation, reproduced in
6.5
below) that
\phi_{0}
and
\phi_{1}
are homotopic by a homotopy
\phi_{t}:{\mathcal{T}}\to{\mathcal{T}}\otimes{\mathcal{T}}
such that
(\pi\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\circ\phi_{t}(v)=\tau
for all
t
.
By the universal property of the pull-back we get a homotopy
\psi_{t}:{\mathcal{T}}\to\bar{\mathcal{T}}
from

s+\iota\kappa{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
to
s+\iota\kappa(j\circ q)

such that
r\circ\psi_{t}=\phi_{t}
and
p\circ\psi_{t}={\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
. By the homotopy invariance of
F
we get

F(s+\iota\kappa{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\simeq F(s+\iota\kappa(j\circ q))\ .

In view of
4.10
and the fact that by (
5.4
)
for
!=\mathrm{splt}

the functor
F
has a left-exact, and hence additive, factorization

\textstyle{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\scriptstyle{L_{h,K,!}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{C}}
\textstyle{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

we conclude that

F(s)+F(\iota\kappa{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\simeq F(s)+F(\iota\kappa(j\circ q))

as morphisms with target
F(\bar{\mathcal{T}})
.
Since

F(\bar{\mathcal{T}})
is a group object we can
cancel
F(s)
and obtain the equivalence

F(\iota\kappa{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\simeq F(\iota\kappa(j\circ q))\ .

Since, as seen above,

F(\iota)
is a monomorphism,
and
F(\kappa)
is an equivalence by stability of
F
(note that
\kappa
is a left upper corner inclusion), we can conclude that

F({\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\simeq F(j\circ q)\simeq F(j)\circ F(q)
as desired.
∎

Remark 6.5
.

For completeness of the presentation, following
[
Fri
]

we sketch the construction of the homotopy between
\phi_{0}
and
\phi_{1}

from (
6.2
).
These homomorphisms are determined via the universal property of
{\mathcal{T}}
by

\phi_{0}(v)=v(1-e)\otimes 1_{{\mathcal{T}}}+e\otimes v\ ,\quad\phi_{1}(v)=v(1-e)\otimes 1_{{\mathcal{T}}}+e\otimes 1_{{\mathcal{T}}}\ .

We will employ an explicit realization of
{\mathcal{T}}
by bounded operators on
L^{2}({\rm Nature})
. If
(\xi_{i})_{i\in{\rm Nature}}
denotes the standard basis, then
v
is the isometry given by
v\xi_{i}=\xi_{i+1}
for all
i
in
{\rm Nature}
. Furthermore,

e
is the projection onto the subspace generated by
\xi_{0}
.

We realize
{\mathcal{T}}\otimes{\mathcal{T}}
correspondingly on
L^{2}({\rm Nature}\times{\rm Nature})
with basis
(\xi_{i,j})_{i,j\in{\rm Nature}\times{\rm Nature}}
. We define
the selfadjoint unitaries

u_{0}:=v(1-e)v^{*}\otimes 1_{{\mathcal{T}}}+ev^{*}\otimes v+ve\otimes v^{*}+e\otimes e

and

u_{1}:=v(1-e)v^{*}\otimes 1_{{\mathcal{T}}}+ev^{*}\otimes 1_{{\mathcal{T}}}+ve\otimes 1_{{\mathcal{T}}}\ .

One then checks that

u_{0}(v\otimes 1_{{\mathcal{T}}})u_{0}^{*}=\phi_{0}(v)\ ,\quad u_{1}(v\otimes 1_{{\mathcal{T}}})u_{1}^{*}=\phi_{1}(v)

in
{\mathcal{T}}\otimes{\mathcal{T}}
.
On basis vectors the unitary
u_{0}
is given by

\xi_{i,j}\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\xi_{0,0}&(i,j)=(0,0)\\
\xi_{1,j-1}&i=0,j\geq 1\\
\xi_{0,j+1}&i=1,j\geq 0\\
\xi_{i,j}&i\geq 2\end{array}\right.

We connect

u_{0}
by a homotopy
u_{0,t}
with
1_{{\mathcal{T}}\otimes{\mathcal{T}}}
by a path in
{\mathcal{T}}\otimes{\mathcal{T}}

which rotates (with constant speed) in the each of the two-dimensional subspaces
{\mathbb{C}}\langle\xi_{1,j-1},\xi_{0,j}\rangle
for
j\geq 1
from flip to the identity.
Similarly, the action of

u_{1}
on basis vectors is given by

\xi_{i,j}\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\xi_{1,j}&i=0\\
\xi_{0,j}&i=1\\
\xi_{i,j}&i\geq 2\end{array}\right.

We connect

u_{1}
by a homotopy
u_{1,t}
with
1_{{\mathcal{T}}\otimes{\mathcal{T}}}
by a path in
{\mathcal{T}}\otimes{\mathcal{T}}

which rotates (with constant speed) in the each of the two-dimensional subspaces

{\mathbb{C}}\langle\xi_{0,j},\xi_{1,j}\rangle
for
j\geq 0
from the flip to the identity.
Then
u_{0,t}^{*}\phi_{0}u_{0,t}
is a homotopy from
\phi_{0}
to the map determined by
v\mapsto v\otimes 1_{{\mathcal{T}}}
. Similarly,
u_{1,t}^{*}\phi_{1}u_{1,t}
is a homotopy from
\phi_{1}
to the same map.
The concatenation of the first with the inverse of the second homotopy is the desired homotopy
\phi_{t}
.
One checks from the explicit formulas, that
(\pi\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\circ\phi_{t}(v)=\tau
for all
t
.

Note that the selfadjointness of
u_{i}
is not relevant here, but it would be important for a version for real
C^{*}
-algebras.
∎

We now consider the split-exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{T}}_{0}\to{\mathcal{T}}\xrightarrow{q}{\mathbb{C}}\to 0

(6.3)

defining

{\mathcal{T}}_{0}
as an ideal in
{\mathcal{T}}
.
As above we consider a functor

F:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to{\mathbf{C}}
to a semi-additive
\infty
-category.

Corollary 6.6
.

If
F
is homotopy invariant, stable, split-exact and takes values in
group objects,
then

F({\mathcal{T}}_{0})\simeq 0
.

Proof.

Since
F
is split-exact it sends the split-exact exact sequence (
6.3
) to a fibre sequence.
Since
F(q)
is an equivalence by
6.4
we conclude that its fibre
F({\mathcal{T}}_{0})
is a zero object.
∎

The Toeplitz sequence (
6.1
) is semi-split exact. This can be seen either by an application of the Choi-Effros lifting theorem
[
CE76
]
using that
K
is nuclear, or by an explicit construction of a cpc right inverse
s
of
\pi
, see
6.7
.

Remark 6.7
.

For completeness of the presentation we provide a cpc split for
the Toeplitz extension (
6.1
). We consider
L^{2}({\mathbb{Z}})
with the standard basis
(\xi_{i})_{i\in{\mathbb{Z}}}

and realize the Toeplitz algebra
{\mathcal{T}}
on the subspace

L^{2}({\rm Nature})
as in
6.5
.
We let
w
be the unitary
shift operator determined by
\xi_{i}\mapsto\xi_{i+1}
for all
i
in
{\mathbb{Z}}
and

P:L^{2}({\mathbb{Z}})\to L^{2}({\rm Nature})
be the orthogonal projection.
Then we have
v=PwP
. Since
C(S^{1})
classifies unitaries (see
4.9
)
we have a unique unital homomorphism
\phi:C(S^{1})\to B(L^{2}({\mathbb{Z}}))

determined by
\phi(u):=w
.
By an explicit calculation of its action on basis vectors one checks that
[P,\phi(u^{n})]
is finite-dimensional and therefore belongs to
K
for all
n
in
{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Since
u
generates
C(S^{1})

we conclude that
[P,\phi(f)]\in K

for all
f
in
C(S^{1})
.
We define the linear map

s:C(S^{1})\to B(L^{2}({\rm Nature}))
by
s(f)=P\phi(f)P
.
Using the discussion above one checks that it takes vales in
{\mathcal{T}}
. Moreover, since
\pi(s(u))=\pi(v)=u

we conclude that
\pi\circ s={\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
. Since it is the compression of a homomorphism
it is completely positive.

∎

Remark 6.8
.

The Toeplitz extension does not admit a split. For this reason in the constructions below we must assume that
!
belongs to
\{\mathrm{se},\mathrm{ex}\}
and exclude the case
\mathrm{splt}
.
∎

We consider the diagram of vertical exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{T}}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{0}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{T}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces S({\mathbb{C}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s_{0}}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces C(S^{1})}
\scriptstyle{s}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0}

(6.4)

where the lower square is a cartesian and the map
i
is an in (
4.10
). The cpc split
s
induces a cpc split
s_{0}
as indicated.

We let
!
in
\{\mathrm{se},\mathrm{ex}\}
and apply the semiexact functor
L_{h,K,!}
to the left vertical semi-split exact sequence in order to
get a fibre sequence

L_{h,K,!}(S^{2}({\mathbb{C}}))\xrightarrow{\beta_{!,{\mathbb{C}}}}L_{h,K,!}({\mathbb{C}})\to L_{h,K,!}({\mathcal{T}}_{0})\to L_{h,K,!}(S({\mathbb{C}}))

(6.5)

defining
\beta_{!,{\mathbb{C}}}
,
where we used

L_{h,K,!}({\mathbb{C}})\simeq L_{h,K,!}(K)
by stability of
L_{h,K,!}
.

Since we want to speak about the two-fold loop functor in different left-exact
\infty
-categories we add subscripts indicating which category is meant in each case.
Note that for all
k
in
{\rm Nature}
, by
3.19
and the left-exactness of
L_{!}\circ L_{K}
the
k
-fold loop functor
\Omega_{!}^{k}
on
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}
can be represented by the
k
-fold suspension
on the level of
C^{*}
-algebras

\Omega^{k}_{!}(-)\simeq L_{h,K,!}(S^{k}({\mathbb{C}}))\otimes-:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}\ .

Recall the tensor unit constraint

{\mathtt{id}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}}\simeq L_{h,K,!}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes-:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}\ .

The following definition implicitly uses these identifications.

Definition 6.9
.

We define a natural transformation of endofunctors

\beta_{!}:=\beta_{!,{\mathbb{C}}}\otimes-:\Omega_{!}^{2}\to{\mathtt{id}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}}:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}

We consider a functor

E:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}\to{\mathbf{C}}
to a semi-additive
\infty
-category and
let
A
be an object of
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}
.

Corollary 6.10
.

If
E
is left-exact and
E(-\otimes A)
takes values in
group objects, then

E(\beta_{!,A}):E(\Omega_{!}^{2}(A))\to E(A)
is an equivalence.

Proof.

We consider the functor

F(-):=E(-\otimes_{\max}A)
.
Then
F(\beta_{!,{\mathbb{C}}})\simeq E(\beta_{!,A})
. Using
5.8
.
2

we observe that
L_{h,K,!}^{*}F
belongs to
{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s,!}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{C}})
. Since it also
takes values in group objects we can apply
6.6
in order to conclude that

F(L_{h,K,!}({\mathcal{T}}_{0}))\simeq 0
.

The functor
F
sends the fibre sequence
(
6.5
) to a fibre sequence

F(\Omega^{2}L_{h,K,!}({\mathbb{C}}))\xrightarrow{F(\beta_{!,{\mathbb{C}}})}F(L_{h,K,!}({\mathbb{C}}))\to F(L_{h,K,!}({\mathcal{T}}_{0}))\ .

Hence

F(\beta_{!,{\mathbb{C}}})
is an equivalence.
∎

Recall that
!
is in
\{\mathrm{se},\mathrm{ex}\}
.

Corollary 6.11
.

If
A
is a group object in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}
, then

\beta_{!,A}:\Omega^{2}_{!}(A)\to A
is an equivalence.

Proof.

We apply
6.10
to the identity functor in place of
E
.
We further use that if
A
is a group object, then so is
B\otimes A
for every
B
in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}
.
∎

The statements of
6.4
,
6.6
,
6.10
, and
6.11
all have separable versions which are obtained by adding subscripts
\mathrm{sep}
appropriately.

7
Group objects and
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}

In this section we consider the full subcategories of group objects in the
semi-additive
\infty
-categories
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}
for
!
in
\{\mathrm{se},\mathrm{ex}\}
and their separable versions.
They are the targets of the two-fold loop functor and turn out the be stable
\infty
-categories.
This two-fold loop functor is the right-adjoint of a right Bousfield localization. It is the last step of the chain of localizations described in
Section

1
.
In the separable case, the composition of all four localizations yields
the functors
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and

\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
whose universal properties will be stated in
(
7.7
)
and (
7.9
).

Dually to the situation described at the beginning of
Section

4
let
{\mathbf{C}}
be an
\infty
-category with an endofunctor
R:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{C}}
and a natural transformation

\beta:R\to{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
. If for every object
C
the morphisms

\beta_{R(C)},R(\beta_{C}):R(R(C))\to R(C)

are equivalences, then
R
is the right-adjoint of a right Bousfield localization with counit
\beta
. The functor
R:{\mathbf{C}}\to R({\mathbf{C}})
is also a Dwyer-Kan localization
at the set of morphisms
W_{R}:=\{\beta_{C}\mid C\in{\mathbf{C}}\}
.
If
{\mathbf{C}}
is left-exact, then the localization
R
is automatically left exact.

If
{\mathbf{C}}
is semi-additive, then we let
{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{group}}
denote the full subcategory of group objects in
{\mathbf{C}}
.
A full subcategory of a semi-additive
\infty
-category which is closed under products is again semi-additive.
A semi-additive
\infty
-category is called
additive
if all its objects are groups. If
{\mathbf{C}}
is semi-additive, then
{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{group}}
is additive.

Example 7.1
.

A stable
\infty
-category is additive.
∎

We consider
!
in
\{\mathrm{se},\mathrm{ex}\}
and
?
in
\{\min,\max\}
allowing the following combinations:

!
\backslash
?

min

max

se

yes

yes

ex

no

yes

We consider the two-fold loop endofunctor
\Omega^{2}_{!}
on
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}
and the natural transformation
\beta_{!}:\Omega_{!}^{2}\to{\mathtt{id}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}}
from

6.9
.

Proposition 7.2
.

1.

The essential image of
\Omega_{!}^{2}
is
L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}}
.

2.

The functor
\Omega_{!}^{2}

is the right-adjoint of a right Bousfield localization with counit
\beta_{!}:\Omega_{!}^{2}\to{\mathtt{id}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}}
.
In addition, the localization
\Omega_{!}^{2}
is left-exact.

3.

The
\infty
-category
L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}}
is stable.

4.

For every left-exact and additive
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
we have an equivalence

(\Omega_{!}^{2}\circ L_{h,K,!})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}(L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s,!+Sch}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

(7.1)

5.

The localization
\Omega_{!}^{2}
admits a symmetric monoidal refinement.

6.

The functor

-\otimes_{?}-:L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}}\times L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}}\to L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}}

is bi-exact.

7.

For every symmetric monoidal, left-exact and additive
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
we have an equivalence

(\Omega_{!}^{2}\circ L_{h,K,!})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}(L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{h,s,!+Sch}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

(7.2)

Proof.

We start with Assertion
1
.
By
6.3
the functor
\Omega_{!}^{2}
takes values in group objects.
If
A
belongs to
L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}}
, then
\beta_{!,A}:\Omega_{!}^{2}(A)\to A
is an equivalence by
6.11
. Hence the essential image of
\Omega_{!}^{2}
is precisely
L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}}
.

For Assertion
2
we first note that

\beta_{!,\Omega_{!}^{2}(A)}
is an equivalence again by
6.11
.
We furthermore employ the symmetry of the tensor product and
\beta_{!,A}\simeq\beta_{!,{\mathbb{C}}}\otimes A
in order to see that

\Omega_{!}^{2}(\beta_{!,A})\simeq\beta_{!,\Omega_{!}^{2}(A)}
is an equivalence, too.

In order to show Assertion
3
we show that the loop functor

\Omega_{!}:L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}}\to L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}}

is an equivalence. Indeed, by
6.11
the restriction of the natural transformation
\beta_{!}:\Omega_{!}^{2}\to{\mathtt{id}}
from
6.9

to group objects is an equivalence which exhibits
\Omega_{!}
as its own inverse.

Assertion
4
follows from

\displaystyle{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}(L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}},{\mathbf{D}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Omega^{2,*}_{!}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!},{\mathbf{D}})

(7.3)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L_{h,K,!}^{*}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s,!+Sch}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

The functor
\Omega_{!}^{2,*}
preserves left-exact functors by Assertion
2
. It is fully faithful and essentially surjective since any left-exact functor from
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}
to an additive category
{\mathbf{D}}
automatically
inverts by
6.10
the set
W_{\Omega^{2}_{!}}:=\{\beta_{A,!}|A\in L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}\}
of generators of the Dwyer-Kan localization
\Omega_{!}^{2}
.
The second equivalence in (
7.3
) is
(
5.4
).

For Assertion
5
we observe that
the equivalence
{\mathtt{id}}_{A}\otimes\beta_{!,B}\simeq\beta_{!,A\otimes B}
for all
A
and
B
in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}
implies that the endofunctor

A\otimes-
of
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}
preserves the set
W_{\Omega^{2}_{!}}
. Consequently,

\Omega^{2}_{!}
admits a symmetric monoidal refinement. Furthermore, for

A
in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}
the endofunctor
A\otimes-
descends to a left-exact endofunctor on

L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}}
. This implies Assertion
6
.

Finally, Assertion
7
follows from

\displaystyle{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}(L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}},{\mathbf{D}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Omega_{!}^{2,*}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!},{\mathbf{D}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L_{h,K,!}^{*}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s,!+Sch}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{D}})\ ,

where the first follows from the left-exactness of
\Omega_{!}^{2}
shown in Assertion
5
, and the second is (
5.6
).
∎

The
7.2
has a separable version which we state for later reference.

Proposition 7.3
.

1.

The essential image of
\Omega_{\mathrm{sep},!}^{2}
is
L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}}
.

2.

The functor
\Omega_{\mathrm{sep},!}^{2}

is the right-adjoint of a right Bousfield localization with counit
\beta_{\mathrm{sep},!}:\Omega_{\mathrm{sep},!}^{2}\to{\mathtt{id}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}}
.
In addition, the localization
\Omega_{\mathrm{sep},!}^{2}
is left-exact.

3.

The
\infty
-category
L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}}
is stable.

4.

For every left-exact and additive
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
we have an equivalence

(\Omega_{\mathrm{sep},!}^{2}\circ L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,!})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}(L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s,!+Sch}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

(7.4)

5.

The localization
\Omega_{\mathrm{sep},!}^{2}
admits a symmetric monoidal refinement.

6.

The functor

-\otimes_{?}-:L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}}\times L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}}\to L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}}

is bi-exact.

7.

For every symmetric monoidal, left-exact and additive
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
we have an equivalence

(\Omega_{\mathrm{sep},!}^{2}\circ L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,!})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}(L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{h,s,!+Sch}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

(7.5)

We note that
L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}}
is a large
\infty
-category while
L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}}
is essentially small and locally small.

Remark 7.4
.

If
{\mathbf{D}}
is in addition stable, then by
5.2
,
in the right-hand sides of (
7.1
), (
7.2
), (
7.4
) and (
7.5
) we can omit the superscript
+Sch
.
∎

Proposition 7.5
.

The functors

\hskip-5.69046pt\Omega_{!}^{2}\circ L_{h,K,!}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}}\ ,\quad\Omega_{\mathrm{sep},!}^{2}\circ L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,!}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}}

are Dwyer-Kan localizations.

Proof.

We write out the details in the separable case. The non-separable case is analogous. The functor
L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,!}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}

is constructed as an iterated Dwyer-Kan localization at sets of morphisms in

C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. By definition, the last step
\Omega_{\mathrm{sep},!}^{2}:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}\to{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}}
is a Dwyer-Kan localization
at the set of morphisms
\beta_{\mathrm{sep},!,A}:L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,!}(S^{2}(A))\to L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,!}(A)
for all
A
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. These morphisms only exist in the localization
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}
.
But we can replace them by a collection of images of morphisms in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. For
A
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
we have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,!}(S^{2}(A))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,!}(\lambda_{A})}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{\mathrm{sep},!,A}}
\textstyle{L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,!}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,!}(\kappa_{A})}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,!}(C(\pi_{A}))}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,!}(A\otimes K)}
\scriptstyle{L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,!}(\iota_{\pi_{A}})}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}

in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}
,
where
\kappa_{A}
is the left upper corner inclusion (
4.1
),
\iota_{\pi_{A}}
is the canonical inclusion is associated to the semi-split exact sequence

0\to A\otimes K\to A\otimes{\mathcal{T}}_{0}\xrightarrow{\pi_{A}}S(A)\to 0

(the tensor product of the left column in (
6.4
) with
A
) as in (
5.1
), and
\lambda_{A}:S^{2}(A)\to C(\pi_{A})
is the canonical inclusion.
Hence
\Omega_{\mathrm{sep},!}^{2}
is also the Dwyer-Kan localization at the collection of morphisms

(L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,!}(\lambda_{A}))_{A\in C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
.
We can conclude that the composition
\Omega_{\mathrm{sep},!}^{2}\circ L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,!}
is a Dwyer-Kan localization.
∎

Definition 7.6
.

We define the
KK
-theory functor for separable
C^{*}
-algebras

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}

(7.6)

to be the functor

\Omega^{2}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{se}}\circ L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,\mathrm{se}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,\mathrm{se}}}^{\mathrm{group}}\ .

So
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is a locally small and essentially small stable
\infty
-category.
The functor
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}
has the universal property that

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}(\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s,se+Sch}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{D}})

(7.7)

for any left-exact and additive
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
.
Since we know by
7.3
.
3
that
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is stable the restriction of this universal property to stable
\infty
-categories
{\mathbf{D}}
(where by
7.4
we can omit the superscript
+Sch
) already characterizes the functor
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
up to equivalence.

Remark 7.7
.

By
[
BEL
, Thm. 1.5]
the functor
denoted by the same symbol in
[
BEL
, Def. 1.2]
(for the trivial group
G
)
has the same universal property and therefore
is canonically equivalent to the functor defined above.
We can conclude by
[
BEL
, Thm. 1.3]
that the functor

\mathrm{ho}\circ\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{ho}\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}

with values in the triangulated category
\mathrm{ho}\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is canonically equivalent to
the classical functor considered in
[
MN06
]
and the
C^{*}
-literature elsewhere. As explained in the introduction in the present paper we will give an independent proof for this fact, see
1.3
.

∎

Remark 7.8
.

Since
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is stable it admits all finite colimits. After some hard work, in
12.3
below we will see that
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
admits countable colimits and is thus idempotent complete. We do not have a direct proof of this fact just from the constructions.

∎

Definition 7.9
.

We define the
E
-theory functor for separable
C^{*}
-algebras

\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}

(7.8)

to be the functor

\Omega^{2}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{ex}}\circ L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,\mathrm{ex}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,\mathrm{ex}}}^{\mathrm{group}}\ .

So
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is a locally small and essentially small stable
\infty
-category.
The functor in (
7.8
) is the inital homotopy invariant, stable and exact functor to a left-exact and additive
\infty
-category, i.e. for every any left-exact and additive
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
we have the equivalence

\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}(\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s,ex+Sch}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

(7.9)

Remark 7.10
.

The justification for calling the functor defined in
7.9
the
E
-theory functor is that it has an analogous universal property as the additive
1
-category-valued
E
-theory functors considered

[
Hig90a
]
,
[
CH90
]
.
In fact, in
Theorem

13.16
we show that after going to the homotopy category the functor

\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}
becomes equivalent to the classical
E
-theory functor for separable algebras.

Asymptotic morphisms will be discussed in
Section

14
below.
∎

Remark 7.11
.

Since
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
are stable, at a first glance it looks more natural
to
formulate the universal properties for stable targets
{\mathbf{D}}
. But we will take advantage of the more general version for left-exact additive
\infty
-categories in
Section

10
below.
∎

8
s
-finitary functors

In this section we extend the
KK
and
E
-theory functors from separable to all
C^{*}
-algebras and
characterize these extensions by their universal properties.

To any essentially small and locally small stable
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
we can associate its
{\mathtt{Ind}}
-completion

y:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathtt{Ind}}({\mathbf{C}})\ .

As a model, using that
{\mathbf{C}}
has mapping spectra, one can take
the Yoneda embedding

y:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}({\mathbf{C}},\mathbf{Sp})\ .

(8.1)

The large stable
\infty
-category
{\mathtt{Ind}}({\mathbf{C}})
is presentable, and the fully faithful and exact functor
y
has the universal property that for any cocomplete stable
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
the pull-back along
y
is an equivalence

y^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}}({\mathtt{Ind}}({\mathbf{C}}),{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}({\mathbf{C}},{\mathbf{D}})\ ,

(8.2)

where the supercript
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}
indicates small colimit preserving functors.
If
{\mathbf{C}}
has a bi-exact symmetric monoidal structure, then
{\mathtt{Ind}}({\mathbf{C}})
has a natural symmetric monoidal structure and

y
has a symmetric monoidal refinement such that for any cocomplete bi-cocontinuous symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
the pull-back along
y^{*}
induces an equivalence

y^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}({\mathtt{Ind}}({\mathbf{C}}),{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}({\mathbf{C}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

(8.3)

The inverse of the restriction is given by left Kan-extension.
In the model (
8.1
) the symmetric monoidal structure on the functor category is the Day convolution structure on the functor category.

Let
!
be in
\{\mathrm{se},\mathrm{ex}\}
and
?
be in
\{\min,\max\}
. As before we allow the following combinations:

!
\backslash
?

min

max

se

yes

yes

ex

no

yes

For the moment we use the abbreviations

\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep},!}:=L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,!}}^{\mathrm{group}}\ ,\quad\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep},!}:=\Omega^{2}_{\sharp,!}\circ L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K,!}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep},!}

(8.4)

instead of
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
or
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}

order to discuss
KK
and
E
-theory in a parallel manner.

Definition 8.1
.

We define
\mathrm{KK}_{!}:={\mathtt{Ind}}(\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep},!})
and the functor

\mathrm{kk}_{!}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathrm{KK}_{!}

as the left Kan-extension

\textstyle{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{incl}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep},!}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep},!}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{y}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{!}}
\textstyle{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{!}}

of
y\circ\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep},!}
along
\mathrm{incl}
.

Since the inclusion functor
\mathrm{incl}
is fully faithful the triangle commutes up to a natural equivalence.

The following properties of the functor
\mathrm{kk}_{!}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathrm{KK}_{!}
are immediate from the definition.

Corollary 8.2
.

1.

We have an equivalence
\mathrm{kk}_{!}\circ\mathrm{incl}\simeq y\circ\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep},!}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{KK}_{!}
.

2.

\mathrm{KK}_{!}
is a large presentable stable
\infty
-category compactly generated by the image of
y
.

3.

The functor
\mathrm{kk}_{!}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathrm{KK}_{!}
is
s
-finitary.

4.

\mathrm{KK}_{!}
admits a bi-cocontinuous symmetric monoidal structure
\otimes_{?}
and
y^{*}
has a symmetric monoidal refinement such that

y^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}(\mathrm{KK}_{!},{\mathbf{C}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\otimes,\mathrm{lex}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}(\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep},!},{\mathbf{D}})

is an equivalence for any cocomplete bi-cocontinuous symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category with
{\mathbf{D}}
.

5.

The tensor product with the image

b:\mathrm{kk}_{!}(S^{2}({\mathbb{C}}))\to\mathrm{kk}_{!}({\mathbb{C}})

(8.5)

of the equivalence
\beta_{!,{\mathbb{C}}}
in
\mathrm{KK}_{!}
induces an equivalence
\Omega^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathtt{id}}_{\mathrm{KK}_{!}}

of endofunctors of
\mathrm{KK}_{!}

Remark 8.3
.

The main point of
8.2
.
5

is that the loop functor on
\mathrm{KK}_{!}
is two-periodic, and that this periodicity is implemented
by the product with an (necessarily invertible) element

in
\pi_{-2}\mathrm{KK}_{!}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbb{C}})\simeq\pi_{0}\mathrm{KK}_{!}(S^{2}({\mathbb{C}}),{\mathbb{C}})
,
where
\mathrm{KK}_{!}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbb{C}})
is the commutative endomorphism ring spectrum of the tensor unit of
\mathrm{KK}_{!}
.
This will be used for the calculation of the ring spectrum in
9.19
.

Proposition 8.4
.

F
inherits the following properties from
F_{\mathrm{sep}}
:

1.

homotopy invariance

2.

stability

3.

!
-exactness for
!
in
\{\mathrm{splt},\mathrm{se},\mathrm{ex}\}
, provided in
{\mathbf{C}}
filtered colimits preserve fibre sequences.

Proof.

This is shown in
[
BEL
, Lemma 3.2]
, see also
8.8
.
The case of
!=\mathrm{splt}
(not discussed in the reference) is analoguous to the case
!=\mathrm{se}
.
∎

Theorem 8.5
.

1.

The functor
\mathrm{kk}_{!}
is homotopy invariant, stable, and
!
-exact.

2.

The restriction along
\mathrm{kk}_{!}
induces for every cocomplete stable
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
an equivalence

\mathrm{kk}_{!}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}}(\mathrm{KK}_{!},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s,!,sfin}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

3.

\mathrm{kk}_{!}
has a natural symmetric monoidal refinement such that restriction anlong
\mathrm{kk}_{!}

induces for every cocomplete symmetric monoidal stable
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
with bi-cocontinuous symmetric monoidal structure an equivalence

\mathrm{kk}_{!}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}}(\mathrm{KK}_{!},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s,!,sfin}_{(\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

Proof.

In order to see Assertion
1
note that

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep},!}
is homotopy invariant, stable, and !-exact by
7.2
.
4
. Since

y
is exact, the composition
y\circ\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep},!}
has these properties, too. The Assertion now follows from
8.4
.

Assertion
2
follows from the commutativity of

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 58.94485pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-42.86098pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.61137pt\raise-27.31793pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern where by
8.4
the inverse of the lower horizontal functor is the left Kan-extension functor along
\mathrm{incl}
.

The functor
\mathrm{kk}_{!}
is defined as a left-Kan extension of a symmetric monoidal functor
y\circ\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep},!}
along another symmetric monoidal functor
\mathrm{incl}
. It therefore (see
[
BEL
, Lem. 3.6]
) has a
lax symmetric monoidal refinement. As shown in
[
BEL
, Prop. 3.8]
this structure
is actually symmetric monoidal.
Assertion
3
now follows from the commutativity of

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 58.94485pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-41.96877pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 82.94485pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{h,s,!}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{D}})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

The inverse of the lower horizontal morphism is the left-Kan extension functor.
It preserves symmetric monoidal functors by same argument as for
[
BEL
, Prop. 3.8]
.
∎

Definition 8.6
.

1.

We define the
KK
-theory for
C^{*}
-algebras by

\mathrm{KK}:=\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{se}}\ ,\qquad\mathrm{kk}:=\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{se}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathrm{KK}\ .

2.

We define the
E
-theory for
C^{*}
-algebras by

\mathrm{E}:=\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{ex}}\ ,\qquad\mathrm{e}:=\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{ex}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathrm{E}\ .

Remark 8.7
.

The universal properties of
KK
and
E
-theory are given by Theorem
8.5
.

Thus
\mathrm{kk}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathrm{KK}
is the universal functor to a cocomplete stable
\infty
-category which is
homotopy invariant, stable, semiexact and s-finitary. The category
\mathrm{KK}
has presentably symmetric monoidal structures

\otimes_{?}
for
?
in
{\min,\max}
, and the functor
\mathrm{kk}
has corresponding symmetric monoidal refinements
which have an analoguous universal property for cocomplete stable test categories with bi-cocontinuous symmetric monoidal structures.

The functor
\mathrm{e}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathrm{E}
is the universal functor to a cocomplete stable
\infty
-category which is
homotopy invariant, stable, exact and s-finitary.
The category
\mathrm{E}
has a presentably symmetric monoidal structure

\otimes_{\max}
, and the functor
\mathrm{e}
has a corresponding symmetric monoidal refinement
which has an analoguous universal property for cocomplete stable test categories with bi-cocontinuous symmetric monoidal structures.

Since exactness is a stronger condition than semiexactness we have a canonical comparison functor fitting in to a triangle

\textstyle{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{e}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{E}}

which commutes up to a natural transformation.
Under certain conditions it induces an equivalence on mapping spaces, see
9.16
for a detailed statement.

If
A,B
are separable
C^{*}
-algebras, then by
8.2
.
1
we have equivalences

\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A,B)\simeq\mathrm{KK}(A,B)\ ,\quad\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A,B)\simeq\mathrm{E}(A,B)\ .

(8.6)

∎

Remark 8.8
.

We apologize for introducing an incompleteness of the presentation by deferring the proof of
8.4
to the reference

[
BEL
, Lemma 3.2]
.
But let us point out that the argument for
[
BEL
, Lemma 3.2]
only employs elementary facts about
C^{*}
-algebras and their tensor products
and not any deeper parts from
KK
-theory. It therefore should be directly accessible for readers
having reached this point of the present paper.
The same applies to the argument that
\mathrm{kk}_{!}
is actually symmetric mononidal (in contrast to being lax symmetric monoidal) which is deferred to
[
BEL
, Prop. 3.8]
.
This argument is also by elementary
C^{*}
-algebra theory, but the case of the maximal tensor product is more involved since it uses
[
BEL
, Lemma 7.18]
which does not seem to be so standard.
∎

9
K
-theory and the stable group of unitaries

Using
E
-theory we can give a simple construction of a highly structured version of the topological
K
-functor for
C^{*}
-algebras. The main goal of this section is to relate this
K
-theory functor with the stable
unitary group functor from (
4.12
).

In order to construct the
K
-theory functor we shall use the following general facts.

Remark 9.1
.

If
{\mathbf{C}}
is a stable symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category with tensor unit
{\mathbf{1}}
,
then the functor
{\mathtt{map}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathbf{1}},-):{\mathbf{C}}\to\mathbf{Sp}
is lax symmetric monoidal.
Since
{\mathbf{1}}
is naturally a commutative algebra object in
{\mathbf{C}}
we get a commutative ring spectrum

R:={\mathtt{map}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathbf{1}},{\mathbf{1}})
. The
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
has then a natural
R
-linear structure.
In particular, its mapping spectra
{\mathtt{map}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C,D)
naturally refine to objects of
{\mathbf{Mod}}(R)
such that the composition is
R
-bilinear.
∎

We will apply this to the symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathrm{E}
. Its tensor unit is given by

{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathrm{E}}:=\mathrm{e}({\mathbb{C}})
.

Definition 9.2
.

We define the commutative ring spectrum
\mathrm{KU}:=\mathrm{E}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbb{C}})
in
\mathbf{CAlg}(\mathbf{Sp})
.

We refer to
9.19
for a justification of the notation. The stable
\infty
-category

\mathrm{E}
becomes a
\mathrm{KU}
-linear stable
\infty
-category. In particular, its mapping spectra
\mathrm{E}(A,B)
naturally belong to
{\mathbf{Mod}}(\mathrm{KU})
.

For the moment we consider the maximal tensor product on
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.
In order to incorporate the minimal tensor product see
9.14
.

Definition 9.3
.

The lax symmetric monoidal topological
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-algebras is defined by

K:=\mathrm{E}({\mathbb{C}},-):C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(\mathrm{KU})\ .

By construction,
K
is homotopy invariant, stable and exact.
Since
{\mathbb{C}}
is separable, the object
\mathrm{e}({\mathbb{C}})
in
\mathrm{E}
is compact. Hence,
s
-finitaryness of
\mathrm{e}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathrm{E}
implies
that the

K
-theory functor is also
s
-finitary.

Recall the stable unitary group functor
{\mathcal{U}}^{s}
from (
4.12
).

Proposition 9.4
.

We have a canonical equivalence of functors

\ {\mathcal{U}}^{s}\simeq\Omega^{\infty-1}K:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{CGroups}(\mathbf{Spc})\ .

(9.1)

Proof.

Using
9.3
, stability of the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{E}
and
3.19

we get an equivalence

{\mathtt{Map}}_{\mathrm{E}}(S({\mathbb{C}}),-)\simeq\Omega^{\infty-1}\mathrm{E}({\mathbb{C}},-)\simeq\Omega^{\infty-1}K(-)\ .

(9.2)

We furthermore have a transformation of
\mathbf{CGroups}(\mathbf{Spc})
-valued functors

\hskip-14.22636pt{\mathcal{U}}^{s}_{\mathrm{sep}}(-)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{vfevsdfvrqfccscsdc}}}{{\simeq}}{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(S({\mathbb{C}}),-)\xrightarrow{\Omega^{2}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{ex}}\circ L_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{ex}}}{\mathtt{Map}}_{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(S({\mathbb{C}}),-)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{fvwiuehviuervwvc}}}{{\simeq}}{\mathtt{Map}}_{\mathrm{E}}(S({\mathbb{C}}),-)_{|C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}}\ ,

(9.3)

where
{\mathcal{U}}^{s}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is the restriction of
{\mathcal{U}}^{s}
to separable algebras.
We now employ the following facts.

Lemma 9.5
.

The composition (
9.3
) is an equivalence.

Lemma 9.6
.

The functor
{\mathcal{U}}^{s}
preserves small filtered colimits and is in particular
s
-finitary.

Combining both results we get the desired equivalence (
9.1
)
by left-Kan extending the equivalence (
9.3
)
along
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
and composing with (
9.2
).
∎

Corollary 9.7
.

The
K
-theory functor
K:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{Sp}
preserves small filtered colimits.

Proof.

We combine
9.6
with
9.4

and two-periodicity.
∎

Remark 9.8
.

Using that classical
E
-theory for separable
C^{*}
-algebras preserves countable sums
[
GHT00
, Prop. 7.1]
one can show using
Theorem

13.16

that
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}
preserves countable sums. This implies by

[
BD24
, 3.17]
that
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}
preserves all countable filtered colimits. Since
\mathrm{e}({\mathbb{C}})
is a compact object of
\mathrm{E}
this would give an alternative argument for the fact that
K
preserves filtered small colimits.
∎

Remark 9.9
.

In the proof of
9.5
we will employ the following general fact about mapping spaces in a Dwyer-Kan localization
\ell:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{C}}[W^{-1}]
of
\infty
-categories. We call an object
C
of
{\mathbf{C}}

colocal for
W

if the functor
{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C,-)
sends the elements of
W
to equivalences.
The following assertion is an easy consequence of the Yoneda lemma.
If
C
is colocal for
W
, then

\ell:{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C,-)\to{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{C}}[W^{-1}]}(\ell(C),\ell(-))
is an equivalence of functors from

{\mathbf{C}}
to
\mathbf{Spc}
.

∎

Proof of
9.5
.

We must show that the composition

{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(S({\mathbb{C}}),-)\xrightarrow{L_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{ex}}}{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,\mathrm{ex}}}(S({\mathbb{C}}),-)\xrightarrow{\Omega^{2}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{ex}}}{\mathtt{Map}}_{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(S({\mathbb{C}}),-)

is an equivalence. Since
S({\mathbb{C}})
represents a group in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,\mathrm{ex}}

and
\Omega^{2}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{ex}}
is by
7.2
a right Bousfield localization at the groups the second morphism is an equivalence.
By
9.9
, in order to show that the Dwyer Kan localization
L_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{ex}}
induces an equivalence of mapping spaces
it suffices to show that
L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K}(S({\mathbb{C}}))
is colocal for
W_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{ex}}
.
Since
{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(S({\mathbb{C}}),-)
is left-exact, by (
5.3
) it suffices to show that

L_{\mathrm{sep},h,K}(S({\mathbb{C}}))
is colocal for
\hat{W}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{ex}}
from (
5.2
).
By
5.1
it suffices to show that

{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(S({\mathbb{C}}),-)
sends exact sequences to fibre sequences.
In view of (
4.12
)
this follows from the following Lemma since

\ell
sends Serre fibre sequences to fibre sequences.

Lemma 9.10
.

If
0\to A\to B\to C\to 0
is an exact sequence in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
, then

U^{s}(B)\to U^{s}(C)
is a Serre fibration with fibre

U^{s}(A)
.

Proof.

This lemma is surely well-known in
C^{*}
-algebra theory. For completeness of the presentation we add a proof.

It is clear from the definition (
4.9
) that
U^{s}(A)
is the fibre of the map
U^{s}(B)\to U^{s}(C)
. In order to show that this map is a Serre fibration
we will solve lifting problem

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{x\mapsto(0,x)}
\textstyle{U^{s}(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[0,1]\times X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U^{s}(C)}

for all compact
X
. By (
2.2
) and (
4.11
) this lifting problem is equivalent to

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 13.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.50002pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\{0\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 37.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{U^{s}(C(X)\otimes B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 72.4944pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-13.0pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{[0,1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.11215pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.3694pt\raise-8.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 37.11215pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{U^{s}(C(X)\otimes C)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

It thus suffices to solve
the
path lifting problems

\textstyle{\{0\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{U^{s}(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[0,1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\gamma}}
\textstyle{U^{s}(C)}

(9.4)

for all surjections
B\to C
.

We call
a path
\sigma:[0,1]\to U^{s}(C)

short if
\sigma(0)=1
and

\|\sigma(t)-1\|<1
for all
t
.
For the moment we
assume that we can lift short paths to paths that start in
1
in
U^{s}(B)
.

Let
\gamma:[0,1]\to U^{s}(C)
be a general path.
Then we can find
n
in
{\rm Nature}
such that the segment
\gamma(i/n)^{-1}\gamma_{|[i/n,(i+1)/n]}
is short for all
i=0,\dots,n-1
(we implicitly reparametrize).
We can now lift
\gamma
inductively.
We are given the lift

u
of
\gamma_{|[0,0]}
. Assume that
have found a lift
\tilde{\gamma}
of
\gamma_{|[0,i/n]}
.
We choose a lift
\tilde{\sigma}
of the short path
\gamma^{-1}(i/n)\gamma_{|[i/n,(i+1)/n]}
and
define an extension of
\tilde{\gamma}
on
[i/n,(i+1)/n]
by
\tilde{\gamma}(i/n)\tilde{\sigma}
.

It remains to solve the lifting problem for short paths. To this end we observe that
the exponential map of
(K\otimes A)^{u}
restricts to
\exp:i(K\otimes A)^{\mathrm{sa}}\to U^{s}(A)
with the partial inverse

\log:\{U\in U^{s}(A)\mid\|U-1\|<1\}\to i(K\otimes A)^{\mathrm{sa}}
.
Since the tensor product preserves surjections,
the
map

C_{0}((0,1])\otimes K\otimes B\to C((0,1])\otimes K\otimes C
and its restriction to anti-selfadjoint elements are surjective.
We
interpret
\log(\sigma)
as element in
i(C_{0}((0,1])\otimes K\otimes C)^{\mathrm{sa}}

and can thus
choose a lift
\widehat{\log(\sigma)}
in
i(C_{0}((0,1])\otimes K\otimes B)^{\mathrm{sa}}
.
Then

\tilde{\sigma}:=\exp(\widehat{\log(\sigma)}):[0,1]\to U^{s}(B)
is the desired lift of
\sigma
.
We have thus shown
9.10
.
∎

This finishes the proof of
9.5

∎

Remark 9.11
.

Using that
S({\mathbb{C}})
is a semi-projective
C^{*}
-algebra on could deduce the path lifting in (
9.4
) from
[
Bla16
, Thm. 5.1]
.
∎

Proof of
9.6
.

In view of
2.5
it suffices to show that
the functor
{\mathcal{U}}^{s}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{CGroups}(\mathbf{Spc})
preserves small filtered colimits.
Since the forgetful functor
\mathbf{CGroups}(\mathbf{Spc})\to\mathbf{Spc}
preserves small filtered colimits and is conservative it suffices to show that the underlying
\mathbf{Spc}
-valued functor of
{\mathcal{U}}^{s}
preserves small filtered colimits.

Let
I
be a small filtered poset,
(B_{i})_{i\in I}
be an
I
-indexed family in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
, and set

B:=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}B_{i}
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
. Then we must show that the canonical maps

\pi_{n}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}\ell U^{s}(B_{i}))\to\pi_{n}(\ell U^{s}(B))

are isomorphisms at all choices of base points and for all
n
in
{\rm Nature}
.
Since taking
homotopy groups/sets on
\mathbf{Spc}
commute with filtered colimits
it suffices to
show that the canonical maps
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}\pi_{n}(U^{s}(B_{i}))\to\pi_{n}(U^{s}(B))
are isomorphisms.
This will be an immediate consequence of
9.12
below applied to
the inclusions
S^{n}\to D^{n+1}
or
\emptyset\to S^{n}
.

For
i,j
in
I
with
i\leq j

let

\phi_{j,i}:(K\otimes B_{i})^{u}\to(K\otimes B_{j})^{u}
and

\phi_{i}:(K\otimes B_{i})^{u}\to(K\otimes B)^{u}
denote the connecting map and the canonical homomorphism.

Let
X
be any compact metrizable space and

Y
be a closed subspace.
We fix
i_{0}
in
I
and assume that we are given a square

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.53471pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-7.01389pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.93227pt\raise 6.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.66669pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{U^{s}(B_{i_{0}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.17924pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.18056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi_{i_{0}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.17924pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-7.53471pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 16.26653pt\raise-26.8125pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{g}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.15077pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.15077pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{U^{s}(B)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

Lemma 9.12
.

There exists
i
in
I
with
i\geq i_{0}
and
h:X\to U^{s}(B_{i})
such that
h_{|Y}=\phi_{i,i_{0}}\circ f
and

\phi_{i}\circ h
is homotopic to
g
rel
Y
.

Proof.

For any
C^{*}
-algebra
A
we set
G^{s}(A):=\{a\in GL_{1}((K\otimes A)^{u})\mid 1-a\in K\otimes A\}
. Then

U^{s}(A)\subseteq G^{s}(A)
and
the polar decomposition provides a retraction
W:G^{s}(A)\to U^{s}(A)
.
There exists a

c
in
(0,1)
such that
\max\{\|a^{*}a-1\|,\|aa^{*}-1\|\}\|\leq c
implies
\|W(a)-a\|\leq 1/10
.
We interpret a map
X\to G^{s}(A)
as a point in
G^{s}(C(X)\otimes A)
.
We will write
\phi_{ij}
and
\phi_{i}
instead of
{\mathtt{id}}_{C(X)}\otimes\phi_{ij}
and
{\mathtt{id}}_{C(X)}\otimes\phi_{i}
.
We use the general fact that a
filtered colimit in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is formed by taking the completion of the pre-
C^{*}
-algebra given by the filtered colimit of underlying sets equipped with the induced algebraic structures, see (
2.1
). We furthermore use that
the (maximal) tensor product preserves filtered colimits, and
that we can calculate norms in a filtered colimit as limits of norms. The last statement says e.g. that
for
h
in
C(X)\otimes K\otimes B_{i}
we have
\|\phi_{i}(h)\|=\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{j\in I,i\leq j}\|\phi_{j,i}(h)\|
.

We use Dugundji’s extension theorem in order to find an extension
f_{0}
of
f
in
(C(X)\otimes K\otimes B_{i_{0}})^{u}
such that
f_{0}-1\in C(X)\otimes K\otimes B_{i_{0}}
. Then
g_{0}:=g-\phi^{u}_{i_{0}}(f_{0})\in C_{0}(X\setminus Y)\otimes K\otimes B
.
We can now find

i_{1}
in
I
such that there exists

r
in
C_{0}(X\setminus Y)\otimes K\otimes B_{i_{1}}
with

\|\phi_{i_{1}}(r)-g_{0}\|\leq c/100
.
We set

f_{1}:=\phi^{u}_{i_{1},i_{0}}(f_{0})+r
.
Then
\|\phi_{i_{1}}(f_{1})-g\|\leq c/100
and hence

\max\{\|\phi_{i_{1}}(f_{1}f_{1}^{*})-1\|,\|\phi_{i_{1}}(f_{1}^{*}f_{1})-1\|\}\leq c/10
.
We can then find
i
in
I
with
i\geq i_{1}
such that

\max\{\|\phi_{i,i_{1}}(f_{1})\phi_{i,i_{1}}(f_{1}^{*})-1\|,\|\phi_{i,i_{1}}(f_{1}^{*})\phi_{i,i_{1}}(f_{1})-1\|\}\leq c/3
.
We define

h:=W(\phi_{i,i_{1}}(f_{1}))

in
U^{s}(C(X)\otimes B_{i})
.
Then

h_{|Y}=\phi_{i,i_{0}}(f)

and

\|\phi_{i}(h)-g\|\leq 1/2
. We get a homotopy

(W((1-s)\phi_{i_{0}}(h)+sg))_{s\in[0,1]}
from
\phi_{i}(h)
to
g
rel
Y

in
U^{s}(B)
.
This finishes the proof of
9.12
.
∎

Remark 9.13
.

Using that
S({\mathbb{C}})
is semi-projective we could deduce
9.12
directly
from the proof of
[
BD24
, Prop. 3.8]
, in particular from the existence of the lift in

[
BD24
, Eq. (3.6)]
.
∎

We have now finished the proof of
9.6
.
∎

The reason for using
E
-theory in order to construct the
K
-theory functor for

C^{*}
-algebras was that then exactness of the latter is true by construction.
We have a canonical transformation

\Omega^{\infty-1}\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},-)\to\Omega^{\infty-1}\mathrm{E}({\mathbb{C}},-)\simeq\Omega^{\infty-1}K(-)\ .

The arguments above work equally well for
KK
-theory (just replace
\mathrm{ex}
by
\mathrm{se}
) and show that the canonical transformation

{\mathcal{U}}^{s}(-)\to\Omega^{\infty-1}\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},-)
is an equivalence.
Using Bott periodicity we can then conclude an equivalence of functors

\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},-)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}K(-):C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{Sp}

(9.5)

As a consequence, the stable
\infty
-category
\mathrm{KK}
also naturally acquires a
KU
-linear structure. Furthermore we conclude:

Corollary 9.14
.

The
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-algebras
K:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(\mathrm{KU})

has a lax symmetric monoidal refinement for the minimal tensor product on
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.

The following is true for both tensor products on
\mathrm{KK}
.
We have a limit-preserving symmetric monoidal functor

\mathcal{K}:=\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},-):\mathrm{KK}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(\mathrm{KU})

such that
K\simeq\mathcal{K}\circ\mathrm{kk}
. Since
\mathrm{kk}({\mathbb{C}})
is compact in
\mathrm{KK}
this functor also preserves colimits. It is the right-adjoint of a symmetric monoidal right Bousfield localization

{\mathcal{B}}:{\mathbf{Mod}}(\mathrm{KU})\leftrightarrows\mathrm{KK}:\mathcal{K}\ .

(9.6)

Definition 9.15
.

The essential image of the left-adjoint
{\mathcal{B}}
in (
9.6
) is called the
\mathrm{UCT}
class.

Equivalently, the
\mathrm{UCT}
-class is the localizing subcategory of
\mathrm{KK}
generated by the tensor unit
\mathrm{kk}({\mathbb{C}})
.

Corollary 9.16
.

If
B
is in the
\mathrm{UCT}
-class, then we have the following assertions:

1.

The natural transformation

-\otimes_{\max}B\to-\otimes_{\min}B
of endofunctors on
\mathrm{KK}
is an equivalence.

2.

The transformation

\mathrm{KK}(B,-)\to\mathrm{E}(B,-)
of functors
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(\mathrm{KU})
is an equivalence.

3.

(UCT) We have an equivalence
\mathrm{KK}(B,-)\simeq{\mathtt{map}}_{{\mathbf{Mod}}(\mathrm{KU})}(\mathcal{K}(B),\mathcal{K}(-))
of functors from
\mathrm{KK}
to
{\mathbf{Mod}}(\mathrm{KU})
.

4.

(Künneth formula)
We have an equivalence
\mathcal{K}(-)\otimes_{\mathrm{KU}}\mathcal{K}(B)\simeq\mathcal{K}(-\otimes B)

of functors from
\mathrm{KK}
to
{\mathbf{Mod}}(\mathrm{KU})
.

Proof.

In all cases the equivalence is induced by an obvious natural transformation.
One argues that the full subcategory of objects
B
in

\mathrm{KK}
for which is transformation is an equivalence is localizing, and that it contains
\mathrm{kk}({\mathbb{C}})
.
∎

Remark 9.17
.

The

\mathrm{kk}
-functor from
8.6
.
1
is not compatible with filtered colimits on the level of
C^{*}
-algebras. It does not even preserve countable sums. The reason is that the functor
y:\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{KK}
does not preserve countable sums.
One could improve on this point by observing that
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}
preserves countable sums
12.3
.
2
, and then
working with the
\aleph_{1}
-Ind-completion instead of the Ind-completion in
8.1
. We refer to

[
BD24
, Sec. 3.4]
where the details of such a construction have been worked out in the case of
E
-theory, see also
[
BEL
, Rem. 3.4]

In the context of the present paper, in order to discuss the relation of the
\mathrm{UCT}
-class with the classical definition, it is better to consider
the separable version
\mathrm{UCT}_{\mathrm{sep}}
defined as a smallest countably cocomplete stable
\infty
-category of
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
(which is known to be countably cocomplete by
12.3
.
1
) containing the tensor unit, see also
[
BLP23
, Sec. 5.5]
.
It is a famous question whether for every nuclear separable algebra
A
we have
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)\in\mathrm{UCT}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

The analogs of the statements of
9.16
in the separable case hold true.

∎

Remark 9.18
.

In the statement
9.16
.
1

we can replace the condition that
B
is in the
\mathrm{UCT}
-class by the condition that
B
is represented by a separable and nuclear
C^{*}
-algebra. Indeed, by definition of nuclearity
the transformation
-\otimes_{\max}B\to\otimes_{\min}B
of endofunctors
of
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is an isomorphism.

Classically it is known that for separable algebras the map from
KK
to
E
-theory
is an isomorphism if the first argument is a nuclear algebra
[
Hig90a
, Thm. 3.5]
.
By stability and
Theorem

13.16
we can conclude that in

9.16
.
2
we
can replace the
\mathrm{UCT}
-condition on
B
by the condition that
B
is represented by a separable and nuclear
C^{*}
-algebra.

∎

Remark 9.19
.

In this remark we justify
9.2
.
By a similar argument as in the proof of
9.6
we get a weak equivalence

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}\ell U(n)\cong{\mathcal{U}}^{s}({\mathbb{C}})

in
\mathbf{Groups}(\mathbf{Spc})
.
The left-hand side is
\Omega^{\infty-1}
of one of the classical versions of the
\mathrm{KU}
-spectrum.

We continue the justification of
9.2
by calculating the ring
\pi_{*}\mathrm{KU}
. Since the homotopy groups of
{\mathcal{U}}^{s}({\mathbb{C}})\simeq\Omega^{\infty-1}K({\mathbb{C}})
are two-periodic, so are the homotopy groups on the left-hand side. We thus deduce the classical Bott periodicity theorem. For an explicit calculation
we use the following additional information from classical topology:

\pi_{i}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}U(n))\cong\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}*&i=0\\
{\mathbb{Z}}&i=1\\
0&i=2\end{array}\right.

in order to conclude that

\pi_{i}\Omega^{\infty}K({\mathbb{C}})\cong\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}{\mathbb{Z}}&i\in 2{\rm Nature}\\
0&i\in 2{\rm Nature}+1\end{array}\right.\ .

(9.7)

We know further that
\pi_{*}\mathrm{KU}
is a ring and that the Bott periodicity is implemented by the multiplication with
the invertible element
b
in
\pi_{-2}\mathrm{KU}\simeq\pi_{0}\mathrm{KK}(S^{2}({\mathbb{C}}),{\mathbb{C}})
from (
8.5
).
Consequently,
b^{-1}
must be a generator of

\pi_{2}\mathrm{KU}
and we get a ring isomorphism

{\mathbb{Z}}[b,b^{-1}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}\pi_{*}\mathrm{KU}\ .

∎

10
K
-theory and the group completion of the space of projections

For a unital
C^{*}
-algebra
A
the abelian group
K_{0}(A)
is classically defined
as the Grothendieck group of the monoid of unitary equivalence classes of projections in
K\otimes A
, where the unitaries belong to the multiplier algebra
U(K\otimes A)
. Thereby the monoid operation is induced by the block sum.
One then observes that the relation of unitary equivalence between projections is equivalent to homotopy.
Using the notation from
4.8
we thus get an isomorphism

\pi_{0}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(A))^{\mathrm{group}}\cong K_{0}(A)\ .

(10.1)

In this section we will show a space-level refinement of this isomorphism.
Unfolding definitions we obtain a natural map

\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(A)\to\Omega^{\infty}K(A)

of commutative monoids in spaces.
Then
10.8
asserts that
this map presents its target as a group completion.
Note that the unitality assumption on
A
is crucial for this statement, see
10.1
.
The modification for general algebras
is formulated as
Theorem

10.7
. Though it looks like an obvious
K
-theoretic statement
its detailed verification is surprisingly long.

Example 10.1
.

Let
X
be a locally compact Hausdorff space which is connected and not compact.
Then we have
\mathrm{Proj}^{s}(C_{0}(X))=\{0\}
. Indeed, a projection

p
in
\mathrm{Proj}^{s}(C(X))
can be interpreted as a
function
p:X\to\mathrm{Proj}^{s}({\mathbb{C}})
. The function

x\mapsto\|p(x)\|
is continuous and takes values in
\{0,1\}
. Since it vanishes at infinity, the assumptions on
X
imply that it vanishes identically.

We know from
9.19
that
K_{0}(C_{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}))\cong{\mathbb{Z}}
, and this contradicts
(
10.1
) whose left-hand side would be the zero group.

∎

The following two statements enable us to study the space of projections within the homotopy theory developed in the present notes. They are the analogs of
9.10
and
9.6
.
Recall from
4.8
that
\mathrm{Proj}(A):=\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}({\mathbb{C}},A)
denotes the topological space of projections in
A
and that
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}(A):=\ell\mathrm{Proj}(A)
is the associated space.

Proposition 10.2
.

For every surjective map
B\to C
of
C^{*}
-algebras the map
\mathrm{Proj}(B)\to\mathrm{Proj}(C)
of topological spaces is a Serre fibration.

Proposition 10.3
.

The functor
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{Spc}
preserves small filtered colimits.

We defer the technical proofs of these statements to the end of the section.

Proposition 10.4
.

The functor
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{CMon}(\mathbf{Spc})
is homotopy invariant, stable, Schochet exact, and
s
-finitary.
It furthermore sends cartesian squares

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D}

in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
with the property that

f
a surjection to cartesian squares.

Proof.

The functor
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(-)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{vwihviuewhviwevewre}}}{{\simeq}}{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}({\mathbb{C}},-)
is homotopy invariant and stable
by definition. It furthermore sends Schochet fibrant cartesian squares to cartesian squares
since
L_{h,K}
does so.

Since the functor
K\otimes-
preserves filtered colimits, it follows from
10.3

that
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(-)\simeq\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}(K\otimes-)
preserves filtered colimits and is in particular
s
-finitary.

Since

\textstyle{K\otimes A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\otimes B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{K\otimes f}
\textstyle{K\otimes C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\otimes D}

is again cartesian and
K\otimes f
is still surjective, the functor
\mathrm{Proj}:=\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}({\mathbb{C}},-)
sends this square to a cartesian square in
{\mathbf{Top}}
which is in addition Serre fibrant by
10.2
.
We now apply

\ell
and get the desired cartesian square

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 22.624pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-22.44276pt\raise ∎

The group completion functor
(-)^{\mathrm{group}}
is defined as the left-adjoint of a
Bousfield localization

(-)^{\mathrm{group}}:\mathbf{CMon}(\mathbf{Spc})\leftrightarrows\mathbf{CGroups}(\mathbf{Spc}):\mathrm{incl}\ .

(10.2)

Recall that to any
C^{*}
-algebra
A
we can functorially associate the split unitalization sequence

0\to A\to A^{u}\to{\mathbb{C}}\to 0\ .

(10.3)

Definition 10.5
.

We define the functor

\mathcal{P}\tilde{\mathrm{roj}}^{s}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{CGroups}(\mathbf{Spc})\ ,\qquad A\mapsto{\mathrm{Fib}}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(A^{u})^{\mathrm{group}}\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}({\mathbb{C}})^{\mathrm{group}})

(10.4)

Remark 10.6
.

Observe that in the definition of
\mathcal{P}\tilde{\mathrm{roj}}^{s}
we take the group completion first and then the fibre.
We can not reverse the order since the group completion does not preserve fibre sequences in general.
∎

We define the natural transformation

e_{h}:\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(-)\simeq{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,K}}({\mathbb{C}},-)\xrightarrow{\Omega^{2}_{\mathrm{ex}}\circ L_{\mathrm{ex}}}{\mathtt{Map}}_{\mathrm{E}}({\mathbb{C}},-)\simeq\Omega^{\infty}K(-)

(10.5)

of functors from
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
to
\mathbf{CMon}(\mathbf{Spc})
.
Since
\Omega^{\infty}K
takes values in groups, by the universal property of the group completion we get the dotted arrow in the commutative diagram
of functors
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{CMon}(\mathbf{Spc})

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 14.80385pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.80385pt\raise
