3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 114.6061pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 61.70497pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 114.6061pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Omega^{\infty}K}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 38.80385pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s,\mathrm{group}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 85.39435pt\raise-8.30554pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.86111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hat{\mathrm{e}}_{h}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 118.58484pt\raise-5.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 127.0575pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ ,

where the down-right arrow is the counit of the adjunction (
10.2
).

We next
construct a natural transformation

\tilde{\mathrm{e}}_{h}:\mathcal{P}\tilde{\mathrm{roj}}^{s}\to\Omega^{\infty}K\ .

(10.6)

Applying the exact functor

\Omega^{\infty}K
to the split-exact unitalization sequence (
10.3
) we get the split fibre sequence

\Omega^{\infty}K(A)\to\Omega^{\infty}K(A^{u})\to\Omega^{\infty}K({\mathbb{C}})

in
\mathbf{CGroups}(\mathbf{Spc})
.
We now form the
diagram of vertical fibre sequences of functors from
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
to
\mathbf{CGroups}(\mathbf{Spc})

\textstyle{\mathcal{P}\tilde{\mathrm{roj}}^{s}(-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mathrm{e}}_{h}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{\infty}K(-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}((-)^{u})^{\mathrm{group}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{\mathrm{e}}_{h}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}K((-)^{u})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}({\mathbb{C}})^{\mathrm{group}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{\mathrm{e}}_{h}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{\infty}K({\mathbb{C}})}

(10.7)

defining
\tilde{\mathrm{e}}_{h}
as the natural extension of the lower square to a map of fibres.

The following is the main theorem of the present section.

Theorem 10.7
.

The natural transformation
\tilde{\mathrm{e}}_{h}:\mathcal{P}\tilde{\mathrm{roj}}^{s}\to\Omega^{\infty}K

is an equivalence.

Before we start with the proof we consider the specialization to unital algebras.

Corollary 10.8
.

For every unital
C^{*}
-algebra
A

the map

\hat{\mathrm{e}}_{h,A}:\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(A)\to\Omega^{\infty}K(A)

(10.8)

presents its target as a group completion.

Proof.

Since the composition

\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(A)^{\mathrm{group}}\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(A^{u})^{\mathrm{group}}\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}({\mathbb{C}})^{\mathrm{group}}

vanishes, by the universal property of the fibre in (
10.4
) we get a canonical morphism

i_{A}:\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(A)^{\mathrm{group}}\to\mathcal{P}\tilde{\mathrm{roj}}^{s}(A)\ .

(10.9)

If
A
is unital, then the identity of
A
canonically extends to a homomorphism
A^{u}\to A
. The composition
\mathcal{P}\tilde{\mathrm{roj}}^{s}(A)\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(A^{u})^{\mathrm{group}}\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(A)^{\mathrm{group}}
provides an inverse of
i_{A}
.
The map in (
10.8
) is then equivalent to

\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(A)\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(A)^{\mathrm{group}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i_{A}}}{{\simeq}}\mathcal{P}\tilde{\mathrm{roj}}^{s}(A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tilde{e}_{h,A}}}{{\simeq}}\Omega^{\infty}K(A)\ ,

where the last equivalence is given by
Theorem

10.7
.
This shows the assertion of
10.8
.
∎

All of the above has a version for separable algebras. The following is the separable version of
Theorem

10.7
.

Proposition 10.9
.

The natural transformation

\tilde{\mathrm{e}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}:\mathcal{P}\tilde{\mathrm{roj}}^{s}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\Omega^{\infty}K_{\mathrm{sep}}
is an equivalence.

Proof of
Theorem

10.7
assuming
10.9
.

We claim that
\mathcal{P}\tilde{\mathrm{roj}}^{s}
is
s
-finitary. Since
\Omega^{\infty}K
is also
s
-finitary
we then obtain the equivalence in
Theorem

10.7
as a left Kan-extension of the equivalence
in
10.9
along the inclusion
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.

In order to see the claim we note that
(-)^{\mathrm{group}}
in (
10.2
) is left adjoint and preserves all colimits. By
10.4

the functor
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s,\mathrm{group}}
is also
s
-finitary.
Finally we use that
the fibre of a filtered colimit of maps in
\mathbf{CGroups}(\mathbf{Spc})
is the filtered colimit of the fibres.
∎

The following result prepares the proof of
10.9
.

Proposition 10.10
.

The functor
\mathcal{P}\tilde{\mathrm{roj}}^{s}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{CGroups}(\mathbf{Spc})
is homotopy invariant, stable and
exact.

Proof.

Homotopy invariance and stability are obvious from the definition. Exactness is much deeper. It can not be concluded simply from the exactness properties of
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}
stated in
10.4
since
the group completion functor does not preserve fibre sequences in general.
The basic insight in our special case is that for unital algebras
A
the group completion of
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(A)
can be expressed by a specific filtered colimit.

We first recall some generalities on group completions following
[
Nik
]
,
[
RW13
]
.
We consider a commutative monoid
X

in
\mathbf{CMon}(\mathbf{Spc})
. For an element
s
in
X
we can form

X_{s}:=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}(X\xrightarrow{+s}X\xrightarrow{+s}X\xrightarrow{+s}\dots)

in
X
-modules. For any
finite ordered set
\{s_{1},\dots,s_{n}\}
of elements in
X
we define inductively
X
-modules

X_{\{s_{1},\dots,s_{n}\}}:=(X_{\{s_{1},\dots,s_{n-1}\}})_{s_{n}}\ .

We choose a
well-ordering on
\pi_{0}(X)
and a representative
s
in
X
for any component. We then define the space

X_{\infty}:=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{S\subseteq\pi_{0}(X)}X_{S}.

(10.10)

Proposition 10.11

(
[
Nik
, Prop. 6]
)
.

If the fundamental group of every component of
X_{\infty}
is abelian, then

X\to X_{\infty}
is equivalent to the underlying map of
X\to X^{\mathrm{group}}
.

An element

t
in
\pi_{0}(X)
is called

cofinal
if for every
s
in
\pi_{0}(X)
there exists
s^{\prime}
in
\pi_{0}(X)
and
n
in
{\rm Nature}
such that
s+s^{\prime}=nt
.
One easily checks that if
t
is cofinal, then
the canonical map
X_{t}\to X_{\infty}
is an equivalence.
In particular if the fundamental groups of all components of
X_{t}
are abelian, then

X\to X_{t}
is equivalent to the underlying map of the group completion
X\to X^{\mathrm{group}}
.

The following results enable us to apply
10.11
to our problem.
For
C^{*}
-algebras
A
and
B
we have the commutative monoid

{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,B)
and can form the space

{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,B)_{\infty}
as in (
10.10
)

Lemma 10.12
.

If
\pi_{0}{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,B)
contains a cofinal element, then
the fundamental groups of the components of
{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,B)_{\infty}
are abelian.

Proof.

In the following we use that
\ell\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(A,K\otimes B)\simeq{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,B)
.
Let
[t]
be a cofinal element in
\pi_{0}{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,B)
represented by a map

t
in
\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(A,K\otimes B)
.
Then

{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,B)_{\infty}\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}({\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,B)\xrightarrow{[t]+-}{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,B)\xrightarrow{[t]+-}\dots)\ .

We consider a component
x
in
\pi_{0}{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,B)_{\infty}
. Then
there exists
k
in
{\rm Nature}
and
f
in the topological space
\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(A,K\otimes B)
contributing to
{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,B)

in the
k
-th stage of the
{\rm Nature}
-indexed diagram above which represents
x
.

We now consider elements
[\gamma]
and
[\sigma]
in
\pi_{1}({\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,B)_{\infty},x)
. We must show that
[\gamma]\circ[\sigma]=[\sigma]\circ[\gamma]
. After going further in the diagram we can assume that
[\gamma]
and
[\sigma]
are represented by loops
\gamma
,
\sigma
at
f
in
\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(A,K\otimes B)
.

By cofinality of
[t]
there exists a map
f^{\prime}
and an integer
n
such that

[f]+[f^{\prime}]=n[t]
in
\pi_{0}{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,B)
.
The
+
-sign in the following denotes choices of block sums.
We know that
\gamma+nt
and
\sigma+nt
viewed as points in

\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(A,K\otimes B)
contributing to
{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,B)
in the
(k+n)
-th stage of the diagram also represent
[\gamma]
and
[\sigma]
.
We now have homotopies

\gamma+nt\sim\gamma+f+f^{\prime}
and
\sigma+nt\sim\sigma+f+f^{\prime}
.
It thus suffices to show that
(\gamma+f)\sharp(\sigma+f)\sim(\sigma+f)\sharp(\gamma+f)
, where
\sharp
denotes concatenation.

Conjugating
\sigma+f
with a two-dimensional rotation of blocks we get a homotopy between

(\gamma+f)\sharp(\sigma+f)
and
(\gamma+f)\sharp(f+\sigma)
. Now

(\gamma+f)\sharp(f+\sigma)
is homotopic to

\gamma+\sigma
.
Using the commutativity of
+
up to homotopy we get a homotopy

\gamma+\sigma\sim\sigma+\gamma
.
By reversing the first part we finally get a homotopy from
\gamma+\sigma
to
(\sigma+f)\sharp(\gamma+f)
.
∎

Corollary 10.13
.

If
\pi_{0}{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,B)
contains a cofinal element, then
the map of spaces

{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,B)\to{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,B)_{\infty}

is equivalent to the underlying map of the group completion

{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,B)\to{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A,B)^{\mathrm{group}}\ .

Let now
A
be a unital
C^{*}
-algebra with unit
1_{A}
.
In the following we show that we can apply
10.13
to
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(A)\simeq{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}({\mathbb{C}},A)
by exhibiting a cofinal component.
Let
e
be a minimal projection in
K
.
Then we define
t_{A}:=[e\otimes 1_{A}]
in
\pi_{0}\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(A)
.
The following lemma is well-known.

Lemma 10.14
.

The element
t_{A}
is cofinal.

Proof.

We consider the separable Hilbert space
H:=L^{2}({\rm Nature})
and let

K:=K(H)
. For
n
in
{\rm Nature}
we let
e_{n}
in
K(H)
denote the projection onto the
n
th basis vector of
H
. We further consider the projection
P_{n}=\sum_{i=0}^{n}e_{n}
.

We consider a component
[p]
in
\pi_{0}\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(A)
with
p
in
\mathrm{Proj}(K\otimes A)
. Then
there exists
n
in
{\rm Nature}
such that
\|p-(P_{n}\otimes 1_{A})p(P_{n}\otimes 1_{A})\|<1/2
.
Using function calculus we get a homotopy between
p
and a projection
p^{\prime}
with
p^{\prime}=(P_{n}\otimes 1_{A})p^{\prime}(P_{n}\otimes 1_{A})
. We then have
[p]=[p^{\prime}]
in
\pi_{0}\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(A)

and with

q^{\prime}:=(P_{n}\otimes 1_{A})-p^{\prime}

we get
[p^{\prime}]+[q^{\prime}]=[P_{n}\otimes 1_{A}]=nt_{A}
.
∎

To any unital
C^{*}
-algebra
A
we can functorially (for unital morphisms) associate the
{\rm Nature}
-indexed diagram

\hat{\mathcal{F}}(A):\quad\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(A)\xrightarrow{-+t_{A}}\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(A)\xrightarrow{-+t_{A}}\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(A)\xrightarrow{-+t_{A}}\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(A)\xrightarrow{-+t_{A}}\dots

of spaces.
We furthermore define the functor

{\mathcal{F}}:=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\rm Nature}}\hat{\mathcal{F}}:C^{\ast}\mathbf{Alg}\to\mathbf{Spc}\ .

(10.11)

We conclude that the natural transformation

\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(-)\to{\mathcal{F}}(-)
of
\mathbf{Spc}
-valued functors is equivalent to the transformation

\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s,\mathrm{group}}
of
\mathbf{CMon}(\mathbf{Spc})
-valued functors after forgetting the commutative monoid structure.

We can now finally show the asserted exactness of the functor
\mathcal{P}\tilde{\mathrm{roj}}^{s}
.
We must show that this functor sends
an exact sequence

0\to A\xrightarrow{i}B\xrightarrow{p}C\to 0

of
C^{*}
-algebras
to a fibre sequence in
\mathbf{CGroups}(\mathbf{Spc})
.
We first
form the square

\textstyle{A^{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\scriptstyle{i^{u}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{B^{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{u}}
\textstyle{C^{u}}

(10.12)

in
C^{\ast}\mathbf{Alg}_{/{\mathbb{C}}}
,
where
\pi
is induced by the canonical projection of the unitalization sequence (
10.3
), the homomorphism
j:{\mathbb{C}}\to C^{u}
is induced by the identity of
C^{u}
, and we do not write the structure maps to
{\mathbb{C}}

given by the projections of the unitalization sequences of
A
,
B
and
C
, respectively, and the identity of
{\mathbb{C}}
. This square is cartesian and
p^{u}
is surjective. Applying the functor
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}
we get a diagram in
\mathbf{Spc}_{/\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}({\mathbb{C}})}
, and by
10.4
a cartesian square

(10.13)

We now apply the functor
{\mathcal{F}}
from (
10.11
) to the square (
10.12
). This amounts to forming a filtered colimit of a diagram of squares of the form (
10.13
).
Since a filtered colimit of cartesian squares in
\mathbf{Spc}
is again a cartesian square and since the forgetful functor
\mathbf{CGroups}(\mathbf{Spc})\to\mathbf{Spc}
detects limits we can conclude that

\textstyle{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s,\mathrm{group}}(A^{u})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{j}(\pi)}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s,\mathrm{group}}(i^{u})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s,\mathrm{group}}({\mathbb{C}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s,\mathrm{group}}(j)}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s,\mathrm{group}}(B^{u})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s,\mathrm{group}}(p^{u})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s,\mathrm{group}}(C^{u})}

is a cartesian square in
\mathbf{CGroups}(\mathbf{Spc})
. Together with its non-written structure maps
it is also a diagram in

\mathbf{CGroups}(\mathbf{Spc})_{/\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s,\mathrm{group}}({\mathbb{C}})}
.
We finally take the fibre of the structure maps to
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s,\mathrm{group}}({\mathbb{C}})
and get
the desired cartesian square (or fibre sequence)

Proof of

.

In order to define an inverse transformation

through a functor

defined on

. Since

is left-exact and additive, the universal property
(

) of
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
10.10
together provide the dotted arrow
in

(10.14)

Furthermore, using
9.3
the pull-back along
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}
induces an equivalence

\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{*}:\mathrm{Nat}(\mathcal{P}\bar{\mathrm{roj}}(-),{\mathtt{Map}}_{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}({\mathbb{C}},-))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Nat}(\mathcal{P}\tilde{\mathrm{roj}}^{s}_{\mathrm{sep}}(-),\Omega^{\infty}K_{\mathrm{sep}}(-))\ .

(10.15)

We define a
point
a_{*}
in
\mathcal{P}\bar{\mathrm{roj}}({\mathbb{C}})
as the image of
{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
under

{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbb{C}})\simeq\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}})\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}})^{\mathrm{group}}\xrightarrow{i_{{\mathbb{C}}},\eqref{qewfiuhquiewfqedqwdewd}}\mathcal{P}\tilde{\mathrm{roj}}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{s}({\mathbb{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{vfdsvdfvhosvsdfvfsdv}}}{{\simeq}}\mathcal{P}\bar{\mathrm{roj}}({\mathbb{C}})\ .

(10.16)

Via the Yoneda Lemma this point determines a natural transformation

\tilde{a}:{\mathtt{Map}}_{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}({\mathbb{C}},-)\to\mathcal{P}\bar{\mathrm{roj}}(-)

of functors from
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
to
\mathbf{CGroups}(\mathbf{Spc})
characterized by
\tilde{a}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}))\simeq a_{*}
.
Its pull-back along
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is a natural transformation

a:=\mathrm{e}^{*}_{\mathrm{sep}}\tilde{a}:\Omega^{\infty}K_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathcal{P}\tilde{\mathrm{roj}}_{\mathrm{sep}}

of functors from
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
to
\mathbf{CGroups}(\mathbf{Spc})
.
We have already a natural transformation

b:=\tilde{\mathrm{e}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}:\mathcal{P}\tilde{\mathrm{roj}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\Omega^{\infty}K_{\mathrm{sep}}

defined by means of the diagram (
10.7
). In view of (
10.15
)
there is an essentially unique natural transformatiom

\tilde{b}:\mathcal{P}\bar{\mathrm{roj}}\to{\mathtt{Map}}_{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}({\mathbb{C}},-)

such that
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{*}\tilde{b}\simeq b
.

The following proposition implies
10.9
asserting that
b
is an equivalence.

Proposition 10.15
.

The natural transformations

a
and
b
are mutually inverse to each other.

Proof.

The assertion follows from the following two lemmas.

Lemma 10.16
.

We have
b\circ a\simeq{\mathtt{id}}_{\Omega^{\infty}K_{\mathrm{sep}}}
.

Proof.

It suffices to show

\tilde{b}\circ\tilde{a}:{\mathtt{Map}}_{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}({\mathbb{C}},-)\to{\mathtt{Map}}_{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}({\mathbb{C}},-)

is equivalent to identity. Via the Yoneda Lemma this map is determined by
the point
\tilde{b}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(a_{*})
in
{\mathtt{Map}}_{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbb{C}})
.
We therefore must show that
\tilde{b}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(a_{*})\simeq\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbb{C}}})
.
In order to verify this equivalence we consider the diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}(j)}
\scriptstyle{{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\mapsto\tilde{a}_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}})^{\mathrm{group}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{{\mathbb{C}}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}\bar{\mathrm{roj}}^{s}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{b}_{{\mathbb{C}}}}
\textstyle{{\mathtt{Map}}_{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbb{C}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}}^{u})^{\mathrm{group}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{\mathrm{e}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,{\mathbb{C}}}}
\textstyle{{\mathtt{Map}}_{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbb{C}}^{u})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}})^{\mathrm{group}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{\mathrm{e}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,{\mathbb{C}}}}
\textstyle{{\mathtt{Map}}_{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbb{C}})}

obtained by merging (
10.7
) with (
10.16
),
where
j:{\mathbb{C}}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{u}
is the inclusion.

The
two upper left horizontal arrows send
{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
to
a_{*}
.
We let
\tilde{a}_{*}
denote its image in
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}}^{u})^{\mathrm{group}}
.
Since
\hat{\mathrm{e}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,{\mathbb{C}}}(\tilde{a}_{*})\simeq j_{*}(\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}))
and
j_{*}
is a monomorphism we can conclude that
\tilde{b}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(a_{*})\simeq\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbb{C}}})
.
∎

Lemma 10.17
.

We have
a\circ b\simeq{\mathtt{id}}_{\mathcal{P}\tilde{\mathrm{roj}}^{s}_{\mathrm{sep}}}

Proof.

It suffices to show for every
A
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
that

\pi_{0}\mathcal{P}\tilde{\mathrm{roj}}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)\xrightarrow{b_{A}}\pi_{0}\Omega^{\infty}K_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)\xrightarrow{a_{A}}\pi_{0}\mathcal{P}\tilde{\mathrm{roj}}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)

is an isomorphism.
In fact, in order to deduce the isomorphism for
\pi_{i}
with
i>0
we apply this result
for
A
replaced by
S^{i}(A)
and use the left-exactness of the functors and the fact that they take values in groups.

For the calculation in
\pi_{0}
introduce the following simplified notation (borrowed from
[
Bla98
, 5.3]
):

K_{0}:=\pi_{0}\Omega^{\infty}K_{\mathrm{sep}}\ ,\quad\tilde{K}_{0}:=\pi_{0}\mathcal{P}\tilde{\mathrm{roj}}^{s}_{\mathrm{sep}}

and

V_{00}:=\pi_{0}\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}^{s}_{\mathrm{sep}}\ ,\quad\tilde{K}_{00}:=\pi_{0}\mathcal{P}\mathrm{roj}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{s,\mathrm{group}}\ .

We form the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\tilde{K}_{0}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{A}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces K_{0}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{A}}
\textstyle{\tilde{K}_{0}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V_{00}(A^{u})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(3)}
\textstyle{\tilde{K}_{00}(A^{u})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{e}_{h,\mathrm{sep}}}
\textstyle{K_{0}(A^{u})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{A^{u}}}
\textstyle{\tilde{K}_{0}(A^{u})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(2)}
\textstyle{V_{00}((A^{u}\otimes K)^{u})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(1)}
\textstyle{\tilde{K}_{00}((A^{u}\otimes K)^{u})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tilde{K}_{00}((A^{u}\otimes K)^{u})}

The vertical maps are
inclusion of summands and
all cells except the lower right commute obviously.
We will show that this cell also commutes.
We can then conclude that

a_{A}\circ b_{A}={\mathtt{id}}_{\tilde{K}_{0}(A)}
.

The transformation

V_{00}\to\tilde{K}_{00}
is the algebraic group completion.
In view of its universal property it
suffices to show that the composition of the two lower cells commutes.

We consider a point
[p]
in
V_{00}(A^{u})

given by map
p:{\mathbb{C}}\to A^{u}\otimes K
.
We first calculate its image
k_{rd}
under the right-down composition. The horizontal map sends it to the point in

\tilde{K}_{0}(A^{u})
given by

a_{A^{u}}(\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}(p))\simeq\tilde{K}_{0}(p)(a_{*})
.
The element
k_{rd}
is the image of
\tilde{K}_{0}(p)(a_{*})
in
\tilde{K}_{00}((A^{u}\otimes K)^{u})
under the map
(2)
.
As seen in the proof of
10.16
the map

\tilde{K}_{0}({\mathbb{C}})\to\tilde{K}_{00}({\mathbb{C}}^{u})
sends
a_{*}
to image of
i_{{\mathbb{C}}}:{\mathbb{C}}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{u}
in
V_{00}({\mathbb{C}}^{u})
under group completion map

V_{00}({\mathbb{C}}^{u})\to\tilde{K}_{00}({\mathbb{C}}^{u})
.
The image
k_{rd}
of
\tilde{K}_{0}(p)(a_{*})
in
\tilde{K}_{00}((A^{u}\otimes K)^{u})
is then the image of

p^{u}\circ i_{{\mathbb{C}}}:{\mathbb{C}}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{u}\to(A^{u}\otimes K)^{u}
in
V_{00}((A^{u}\otimes K)^{u})
under the map
(1)
.

We now calculate the image
k_{dr}
of
p
under the down-right composition.
The image of
p
under
(3)

is
i_{A^{u}\otimes K}\circ p
in
V_{00}((A^{u}\otimes K)^{u})
. We now use that
the following square commutes:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 18.96819pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.15915pt\raise 5.81596pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.81596pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{i_{{\mathbb{C}}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 59.21416pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-15.83333pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.33334pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 59.21416pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{C}}^{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 68.4393pt\raise-15.83333pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.90474pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p^{u}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 68.4393pt\raise-23.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-18.96819pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A^{u}\otimes K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 18.87373pt\raise-25.34096pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.30624pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{i_{A^{u}\otimes K}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.96819pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 42.96819pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(A^{u}\otimes K)^{u}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

Consequently we have
i_{A^{u}\otimes K}\circ p\simeq p^{u}\circ i_{{\mathbb{C}}}
in
V_{00}((A^{u}\otimes K)^{u})
.
This implies that
k_{rd}\simeq k_{dr}
.
∎

This finishes the proof of
10.15
.
∎

We finally have completed the proof of
10.9
.
∎

We finish this section with the proofs of the two technical results
10.2
and

10.3
.

Proof of
10.2
.

The statement of
10.2
is surely a known fact in
C^{*}
-algebra theory. But for the sake of completeness we will provide an argument.

We start with two facts taken from
[
Bla98
, Sec. 4.3]
.
We consider a
C^{*}
-algebra
B
and a projection
p
in
B
. Note that projections are always assumed to be selfadjoint.
Assume that
b
is an invertible element in the multiplier algebra
M(B)
and
b=ur
its polar decomposition.

Lemma 10.18
.

If
bpb^{-1}
is a projection, then
bpb^{-1}=upu^{*}
.

Proof.

By assumption

q:=bpb^{-1}
is a projection, so it is in particular selfadjoint.
Writing

q=urpr^{-1}u^{*}
we see that

u^{*}qu=rpr^{-1}
is selfadjoint, too. This implies

rpr^{-1}=r^{-1}pr
. We
multiply with
r
from left and right
and get the equality
r^{2}p=pr^{2}
.
We now use that
r
is positive and therefore
r=\sqrt{r^{2}}
.
We conclude that
also

rp=pr
holds, and this implies

q=upu^{*}
.
∎

In the following we let
B(b,r)
denote the open
r
-ball at
b
in
B
.

Lemma 10.19
.

There exists a constant
c
in
(0,1/2)
and a map

w_{p}:\mathrm{Proj}(B)\cap B(p,c)\to U(B^{u})\cap B(1,1)

such that

w_{p}(q)pw_{p}(q)^{-1}=q

for all
q
in
\mathrm{Proj}(B)\cap B(p,c)
.

Proof.

We define
v_{p}(q):=\frac{1}{2}((2q-1)(2p-1)+1)

and note that

v_{p}(q)\in B^{u}
. If
\|p-q\|<1/2
, then

\|v_{p}(q)-1\|<1
and
v_{p}(q)
is invertible.
Furthermore we have
v_{p}(p)=1
and

v_{p}(q)pv_{p}^{-1}(q)=q
.
We now form the polar decomposition
v_{p}(q)=w_{p}(q)r_{p}(q)
in
B^{u}
.
By
10.18
we then have
w_{p}(q)pw_{p}(q)^{-1}=q
.
By continuity of
w_{p}
and
w_{p}(p)=1
we can find a constant
c
in
(0,1)
such that

w_{p}(B(p,c))\subseteq B(1,1)
.
∎

We consider a surjection
B\to C
of
C^{*}
-algebras.
We must show that the induced map

\mathrm{Proj}(B)\to\mathrm{Proj}(C)
is a Serre fibration. To this end
we will solve the lifting problem

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{x\mapsto(0,x)}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Proj}(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[0,1]\times X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Proj}(C)}

for all compact spaces
X
.
This problem is equivalent to the lifting problem

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 13.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.50002pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\{0\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 37.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Proj}(C(X)\otimes B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 76.06493pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-13.0pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{[0,1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.11217pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 57.0493pt\raise-8.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 37.11217pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Proj}(C(X)\otimes C)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

We must therefore solve the path lifting problem

\textstyle{\{0\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Proj}(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[0,1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\gamma}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Proj}(C)}

for all surjections
B\to C
.

A path
\sigma:[0,1]\to\mathrm{Proj}(C)
is called short if
\|\sigma(t)-\sigma(0)\|<c
and
\|w_{\sigma(0)}(\sigma(t))-1\|<1
for all
t
in
[0,1]
(with
c
and
w_{-}(-)
from
10.19
) .
For the moment we assume that we have a solution for the lifting problem

\textstyle{\{0\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\sigma}(0)}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Proj}(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[0,1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\sigma}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Proj}(C)}

(10.17)

for all short paths
\sigma
.

By continuity and compactness of the interval there exists
n
in
{\rm Nature}
such that
\gamma_{|[i/n,(i+1)/n]}
is short for all
i=0,\dots,n-1
.
We then solve lifting problem inductively by solving the lifting problem for the short paths
\gamma_{|[i/n,(i+1)/n]}
with initial
\tilde{\gamma}(i/n)
.

We finally solve the lifting problem (
10.17
) for short paths
\sigma
.
We set
u(t):=w_{\sigma(0)}(\sigma(t)
. Then

u(0)=1
,
\sigma(t)=u(t)\sigma(0)u(t)^{*}
, and

\|u(t)-1\|<1
.
We get a
path
\log u:[0,1]\to iC^{\mathrm{sa}}
with
\log u(0)=0
.
We interpret

\log u
as an element
i(C_{0}((0,1])\otimes C)^{\mathrm{sa}}
.
Since
C_{0}((0,1])\otimes B\to C_{0}((0,1])\otimes C
is surjective
we can find a preimage
b
in
i(C_{0}((0,1])\otimes B)^{\mathrm{sa}}
.
We then set
v:=\exp(b):[0,1]\to U(B^{u})
. Then

\tilde{\sigma}(t):=v(t)\tilde{\sigma}(0)v(t)^{*}
is the desired lift of
\sigma
with initial
\tilde{\sigma}(0)
in (
10.17
).
∎

Remark 10.20
.

Using that
{\mathbb{C}}
is a semi-projective
C^{*}
-algebra on could deduce the path lifting in (
10.17
) from
[
Bla16
, Thm. 5.1]
.
∎

Proof of
10.3
.

Similarly as in the proof of
9.6
we reduce the assertion to a consideration of homotopy groups and eventually to the following lemma.

We consider a small filtered family
(B_{i})_{i\in I}
of
C^{*}
-algebras indexed by a poset
and set
B:=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}B_{i}
.
For
i,j
in
I
,
i\leq j
we ket

\phi_{j,i}:B_{i}\to B_{j}
be the structure map and

\phi_{i}:B_{i}\to B
be the canonical homomorphism.

Let
X
be a compact metrizable space and

Y
be a closed subspace. Let

i_{0}
be in
I
and assume that we are
given a square

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.53471pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-7.01389pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.93227pt\raise 6.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.66669pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Proj}(B_{i_{0}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.74977pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.18056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi_{i_{0}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.74977pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-7.53471pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 16.26653pt\raise-26.8125pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{g}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.15077pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.15077pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Proj}(B)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

Lemma 10.21
.

There exists
i
in
I
with
i\geq i_{0}
and
h:X\to\mathrm{Proj}(B_{i})
such that
h_{|Y}=\phi_{i,i_{0}}\circ f
and

\phi_{i}\circ h
is homotopic to
g
rel
Y
.

Proof.

Let
A
be any
C^{*}
-algebra. For a selfadjoint element
q
in
A
we let
\sigma(q)
denote the spectrum of
q
. We fix a number

c
in
(0,\frac{1}{2})
and
consider the subspace

P(A):=\{q\in A^{\mathrm{sa}}\mid d(\sigma(q),\frac{1}{2})>c\}

of selfadjoints in
A
with a spectral gap at
\frac{1}{2}
. We observe that
it contains the space of projections
\mathrm{Proj}(A)
.
We fix a
function
\chi\in C({\mathbb{R}})
with
\chi_{|(-\infty,\frac{1}{2}-c]}\equiv 0
and
\chi_{|[\frac{1}{2}+c,\infty)}\equiv 1
.
The map
q\mapsto\chi(q)
defined using the function calculus is a retraction
W:P(A)\to\mathrm{Proj}(A)
.
By continuity we can choose a
constant
c_{1}
in
(0,\infty)
such that
\|q^{2}-q\|\leq c_{1}
implies
\|W(q)-q\|\leq c
.

We interpret
f
as a function
Y\to B_{i_{0}}^{\mathrm{sa}}
.
Using Dugundji’s extension theorem
we find an extension
h_{0}:X\to B_{i_{0}}^{\mathrm{sa}}
.

We then have
g-\phi_{i_{0}}(h_{0})\in(C_{0}(X\setminus Y)\otimes B)^{\mathrm{sa}}
.
We can thus find
i_{1}
in
I
with
i_{1}\geq i_{0}
such that
there exists
r
in
(C_{0}(X\setminus Y)\otimes B_{i_{1}})^{\mathrm{sa}}

with

\|g-\phi_{i_{0}}(h_{0})-\phi_{i_{1}}(r)\|\leq c_{1}/20
.
We set
h_{1}:=\phi_{i_{1},i_{0}}(h_{0})+r
in
(C(X)\otimes B_{i_{1}})^{\mathrm{sa}}
.
Since
g
is a projection, we have

\|\phi_{i_{1}}(h_{1})^{2}-\phi_{i_{1}}(h_{1})\|\leq c_{1}/2
.
We now find

i
in
I
with
i\geq i_{1}
such that
such that

\|\tilde{h}^{2}-\tilde{h}\|\leq c_{1}
, where
\tilde{h}:=\phi_{i,i_{1}}(h_{1})
.

We finally define

h:=W(\tilde{h})
in

\mathrm{Proj}^{s}(C(X)\otimes B_{i})
. By construction we have

h_{|Y}=\phi_{i,i_{0}}(f)
and

\|\phi_{i}(h)-g\|\leq c
. Then

(W((1-s)\phi_{i}(h)+sg))_{s\in[0,1]}
is a homotopy from
\phi_{i}(h)
to
g
rel
Y
.
This finishes the proof of
10.21

∎

Remark 10.22
.

Using that
{\mathbb{C}}
is semi-projective we could deduce
10.21
directly
from the proof of
[
BD24
, Prop. 3.8]
, in particular from the existence of the lift in

[
BD24
, Eq. (3.6)]
.
∎

Hence we have completed the proof of
10.3
.
∎

11
The
q
-construction

The
q
-construction
introduced by Cuntz
[
Cun87
]
is an effective tool to capture Kasparov modules in terms of homomorphisms, see
11.12
. Using the
q
-construction one can express the classical
KK
-theory groups in terms of homotopy classes of maps.
The crucial formula states that
for two separable
C^{*}
-algebras

A
and
B
we have

\pi_{0}\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(qA,K\otimes B)\cong\mathrm{KK}^{\mathrm{class}}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(A,B)\ .

(11.1)

In
[
Cun87
, Def. 1.5]
this isomorphism is actually the definition of the right-hand side. From this formula the composition

\mathrm{KK}^{\mathrm{class}}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(A,B)\otimes\mathrm{KK}^{\mathrm{class}}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(B,C)\to\mathrm{KK}^{\mathrm{class}}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(A,C)

is not obvious. But one can show that the left-hand side in (
11.1
) is naturally isomorphic to

\pi_{0}\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(K\otimes qA,K\otimes qB)
and this makes the composition obvious.

The final goal of the present section and
Section

12
together
is to give a selfcontained proof that for all separable
C^{*}
-algebras
A
and
B

\pi_{0}\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(qA,K\otimes B)\cong\pi_{0}\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A,B)\ .

(11.2)

Remark 11.1
.

The comparison of (
11.1
) and (
11.2
) provides a proof of the
KK
-theory version of
Theorem

13.16
below
which does not depend on the knowledge of the universal property of
\mathrm{kk}^{\mathrm{class}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.
∎

We will start this section with recalling the
q
-construction.
We then continue to study those of its homotopical properties
that are easily accessible without going deeper into
C^{*}
-algebra theory.
We shall see that inverting the images in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
of the canonical morphisms
\iota_{A}:qA\to A
for all separable
C^{*}
-algebras
produces a Dwyer-Kan localization of

L_{q}:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,q}

which is equivalent to composition of the localizations
L_{\mathrm{splt}}
from
5.6
(enforcing split-exactness) and the right Bousfield localization at the subcategory of group objects, see

11.8
and
11.9
.

All of the above has a separable version.
At the end of the present section we go deeper into
C^{*}
-algebra theory. In
Theorem

11.13
we reproduce the proof of
[
Cun87
, Thm. 1.6]
.
As a consequence, for separable
C^{*}
-algebras
A
and
B
we can simplify the formula for the mapping spaces in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,q}
to

\ell\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(qA,K\otimes B)\simeq{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,q}}(A,B)

(11.3)

which is already very close to (
11.2
).
The final step towards this formula, discussed in
Section

12
, is to show that the canonical functor
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,q}\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is an equivalence.

We now start with the description of Cuntz’
q
-construction.
To every
C^{*}
-algebra
A
we can functorially associate
a diagram

126.26727pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 126.26727pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces A\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 105.21751pt\raise-7.88751pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\iota_{i}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 90.40009pt\raise-24.0013pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 168.76727pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 168.76727pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-62.66663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 35.66145pt\raise-62.66663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 80.29509pt\raise-62.66663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 132.51727pt\raise-62.66663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 171.26727pt\raise-62.66663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ ,

(11.4)

where the horizontal sequences are exact.
Recall that the free product
A*A
together with the two canonical maps
\iota_{i}:A\to A*A
,
i=0,1
represents the coproduct in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
. The map
d
(often called the fold map) is determined via the universal property of the free product by
d\circ\iota_{i}={\mathtt{id}}_{A}
for
i=0,1
. The
C^{*}
-algebra
qA
is defined as the kernel of
d
.
The two maps
\iota_{i}
determine splits of the exact sequence.
The maps

p_{i}:A*A\to A
are determined by the conditions

p_{i}\circ\iota_{i}={\mathtt{id}}_{A}
and
p_{i}\circ\iota_{1-i}=0
. We can then define the map

\iota_{A}:=p_{0}\circ i:qA\to A
.
The flip of the two factors of the free product defines an automorphism
\sigma:A*A\to A*A
. Since
d\circ\sigma=d

it restricts to an involutive automorphism
\bar{\sigma}:qA\to qA
.
In principle we should add an index
A
also to the notation for the maps
d,i,\sigma,\dots
as they are all components of natural transformations but we refrain from doing so in order to shorten the notation.

Since
qA
is an ideal in
A*A
we have a canonical map
m:A*A\to M(qA)
, where
M(qA)
denotes the multiplier algebra of
qA
.
We define
m_{i}:=m\circ\iota_{i}
.

Remark 11.2
.

Let
B
be any
C^{*}
-algebra. In order to give a map

f:qA\to B
we could give a map
\hat{f}:A*A\to M(B)
and set

f:=\hat{f}\circ i
. We must ensure that this composition takes values in the ideal
B
of
M(B)
. To this end
we consider the
components

\hat{f}_{i}:=\hat{f}\circ\iota_{i}
of
\hat{f}
.
We must require that
\hat{f}_{1}(a)-\hat{f}_{0}(a)\in B
for all
a
in
A
. Under this condition
f
is a well-defined homomorphism with values in
B
. Moreover, if

\hat{f}_{0}=\hat{f}_{1}
, then it follows that
f=0
.
We will call
\hat{f}
also the
associated homomorphism
.

This construction can be reversed. Assume that
f:qA\to B
is a homomorphism. We define
B^{\prime}:=f(qA)
and the map
\hat{f}:A*A\to M(qA)\xrightarrow{M(f)}M(B^{\prime})
, where we must restrict the codomain of
f
to
B^{\prime}
in order be able to apply the multiplier algebra functor
M
which is only functorial for non-degenerate morphisms. The components of
\hat{f}
are then given by
\hat{f}_{i}:=\hat{f}\circ\iota_{i}:A\to M(B^{\prime})
.
The datum

A\rightrightarrows^{\hat{f}_{0}}_{\hat{f}_{1}}M(B^{\prime})\triangleright B^{\prime}\to B

is called a pre-quasihomomorphism in
[
Cun87
]
.

∎

The functor
q:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is continuous with respect to the topological enrichment of
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
. It therefore preserves homotopy equivalences and descends to a functor
q:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
. Since
q
does not preserve
K
-stability we are led to define the functor

q^{s}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\ ,\quad A\mapsto K\otimes qA\ .

(11.5)

In the following we use the notation

(-)^{s}:=L_{K}(-):C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}

(11.6)

for the stabilization functor from
4.3
.
1
.
We define the natural transformation
\iota^{s}:q^{s}\to(-)^{s}
such that its component at
A
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
is given by

\iota^{s}_{A}:q^{s}A=K\otimes qA\xrightarrow{{\mathtt{id}}_{K}\otimes\iota_{A}}K\otimes A=A^{s}\ .

(11.7)

Recall from
4.4
that
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
is semi-additive. By
6.1
every object in this category is naturally a commutative monoid object.
For
A
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
the object
q^{s}A=K\otimes qA
is thus a commutative monoid object of
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
. We furthermore
define
\bar{\sigma}^{s}:={\mathtt{id}}_{K}\otimes\bar{\sigma}:q^{s}A\to q^{s}A
, where
\bar{\sigma}
is as in (
11.4
).

The following is a version of
[
Cun87
, Prop. 1.4]
.

Lemma 11.3
.

For
A
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
the object

q^{s}A
is a commutative group in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
whose inversion map is given by
\bar{\sigma}^{s}
.

Proof.

We must show that
{\mathtt{id}}_{q^{s}A}+\bar{\sigma}^{s}\simeq 0
. We have the following chain of equivalences

\displaystyle{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(q^{s}A,q^{s}A)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle{\mathtt{Map}}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(K\otimes qA,K\otimes qA)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle{\mathtt{Map}}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(qA,K\otimes qA)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{erigjeroferferfrefwerf}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\ell\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(qA,K\otimes qA)\ .

The first reflects the definition of
q^{s}A
and that

L_{K}
is a left Bousfield localization
4.3
.
1
. The second equivalence is induced by
left upper corner inclusion
qA\to K\otimes qA
which induces an equivalence
since
K\otimes qA
is a local object in this localization.
Under this equivalence the sum
is determined by the block sum of morphisms
qA\to K\otimes qA
.
Hence
{\mathtt{id}}_{q^{s}A}+\bar{\sigma}^{s}
is induced by the composition

{\mathtt{diag}}({\mathtt{id}}_{qA},\bar{\sigma}):qA\to{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(qA)\to K\otimes qA\ .

It suffices to show that

{\mathtt{diag}}({\mathtt{id}}_{qA},\bar{\sigma}):qA\to{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(qA)
is homotopic to zero.

The composition
m\circ i:qA\to M(qA)
is the inclusion, hence it has the associated homomorphism
\widehat{{\mathtt{id}}_{qA}}=m:A*A\to M(qA)
(using the notation introduced in
11.2
) and the components

{\widehat{{\mathtt{id}}_{qA}}}_{\ i}=m_{i}:A\to M(qA)
.
Furthermore, the associated homomorphism
\widehat{\bar{\sigma}}=i\circ\sigma
of
\bar{\sigma}
has the components

{\widehat{\bar{\sigma}}}_{\ i}=m_{1-i}
.
We identify
M({\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(qA))\cong{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(M(qA))
in the natural way.
Then
{\mathtt{diag}}({\mathtt{id}}_{qA},\bar{\sigma})
has the associated homomorphism

\widehat{{\mathtt{diag}}({\mathtt{id}}_{qA},\bar{\sigma})}={\mathtt{diag}}(m,m\circ\sigma):A*A\to M({\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(qA))

and the components

{\widehat{{\mathtt{diag}}({\mathtt{id}}_{qA},\bar{\sigma})}}_{i}={\mathtt{diag}}(m_{i},m_{1-i}):A\to{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(M(qA))
.

For
t
in
[0,1]
we consider the scalar unitary

U_{t}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\cos(\pi t/2)&-\sin(\pi t/2)\\
\sin(\pi t/2)&\cos(\pi t/2)\end{array}\right)

in

{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(M(qA))
. Since
m_{0}(a)-m_{1}(a)\in qA
for all
a
in
A

we have

U_{t}{\mathtt{diag}}(m_{1}(a),m_{0}(a))U_{t}^{*}-{\mathtt{diag}}(m_{0}(a),m_{1}(a))\in{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(qA)

for all
t
in
[0,1]
.
Hence for every
t
in
[0,1]
, as explained in
11.2
, the pair

{\mathtt{diag}}(m_{0},m_{1}),U_{t}{\mathtt{diag}}(m_{1},m_{0})U_{t}^{*}:A\to{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(M(qA))

gives the components of
a map
h_{t}:qA\to{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(qA)
. We have

h_{0}={\mathtt{diag}}({\mathtt{id}}_{qA},\bar{\sigma})
and

h_{1}
is the map with equal components

(\hat{h}_{1})_{i}={\mathtt{diag}}(m_{0},m_{1})
for
i=0,1
.
Hence
h_{1}=0
.
∎

Let
E:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\to{\mathbf{C}}
be a left-exact functor to a semi-additive
\infty
-category.
We say that that
E
is split-exact if it sends the images under
L_{h,K}
of split-exact sequences of
C^{*}
-algebras to fibre sequences.

The following lemma is a version of
[
Cun87
, Prop. 3.1.b]
. Let
A
be a
K
-stable
C^{*}
-algebra. We will use the same symbol for the corresponding object of
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
, i.e., we will write
A
instead of
L_{h,K}(A)
in order to simplify the notation.

Lemma 11.4
.

If
E
is split-exact and
E(A)
is a group, then
the map
\iota^{s}_{A}:q^{s}A\to A^{s}
from (
11.7
) induces an equivalence
E(\iota^{s}_{A}):E(q^{s}A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}E(A^{s})
.

Proof.

The middle horizontal exact sequence in
(
11.4
) is split and induces the fibre sequence

E(q^{s}A)\xrightarrow{E(i^{s})}E((A*A)^{s})\xrightarrow{E(d^{s})}E(A^{s})\ .

(11.8)

Since
A
is
K
-stable we have
A\simeq A^{s}
and hence
E(A)\simeq E(A^{s})
.
In view of our assumptions

E(A^{s})
is a group. Since
q^{s}A
is a group by
11.3
and

E
preserves products (since it is split-exact) we see that also
E(q^{s}A)
is a group. Finally, by
4.5
we have an equivalence

E((A*A)^{s})\xrightarrow{E(p^{s}_{0})\times E(p^{s}_{1})}E(A^{s})\times E(A^{s})

whose inverse is

E(\iota^{s}_{0})\circ{\mathtt{pr}}_{0}+E(\iota^{s}_{1})\circ{\mathtt{pr}}_{1}
.
This implies that that
E((A*A)^{s})
is a group as well.
Using that the objects in the sequence (
11.8
) are commutative groups we see that

E(q^{s}A)\times E(A^{s})\xrightarrow{E(i^{s}_{A})+E(\iota_{1})}E((A*A)^{s})

is an equivalence.
Hence

\hskip-5.69046pt\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}E(\iota^{s}_{A})&0\\
E(p^{s}_{1}\circ i^{s})&{\mathtt{id}}_{E(A^{s})}\end{array}\right):E(q^{s}A)\times E(A^{s})\xrightarrow{E(i^{s})+E(\iota^{s}_{1})}E((A*A)^{s})\xrightarrow{E(p^{s}_{0})\times E(p^{s}_{1})}E(A^{s})\times E(A^{s})

is an equivalence.
Again using that the factors are groups this implies that
E(\iota^{s}_{A})
is an equivalence.
∎

We consider the set of morphisms

\hat{W}_{q}:=\{\iota^{s}_{A}:q^{s}A\to A^{s}\mid A\in C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\}

(11.9)

in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
.

Definition 11.5
.

We define the Dwyer-Kan localization

L_{q}:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,q}

(11.10)

at the set
\hat{W}_{q}
.

We consider the composition

L_{h,K,q}:\>\>\>C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\xrightarrow{L_{h}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\xrightarrow{L_{K}}L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\xrightarrow{L_{q}}L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,q}\ .

(11.11)

Proposition 11.6
.

The functor
L_{h,K,q}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,q}
is a Dwyer-Kan localization.

Proof.

This follows from the fact that
L_{h,K,q}
is a composition of Dwyer-Kan localizations which are
all determined by images of collections of morphisms in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
, namely homotopy equivalences, left upper corner inclusions
\kappa_{A}
from (
4.1
), and the morphisms
{\mathtt{id}}_{K}\otimes\iota_{A}:K\otimes qA\to K\otimes A

for all
A
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.
∎

In
11.8
.
2
we will show that the mapping spaces in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,q}
can be easily understood in terms of a calculus of fractions. In a sense this result is of intermediate nature since
in
11.14
and
11.15
we will state a much better result at the cost of using more of
C^{*}
-algebra theory.

For every
A
in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
we consider the diagram
W(A):{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}

\dots\xrightarrow{\iota^{s}_{q^{3}A}}(q^{3}A)^{s}\xrightarrow{\iota^{s}_{q^{2}A}}(q^{2}A)^{s}\xrightarrow{\iota^{s}_{qA}}(qA)^{s}\xrightarrow{\iota^{s}_{A}}A^{s}\ .

(11.12)

We furthermore let

W_{q}
be the subcategory of
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
generated by
\hat{W}_{q}
.
The diagram
W(A)
is a
putative right calculus of fractions at
A
for
W_{q}
in the sense of
[
Cis19
, Def. 7.2.2]
. In fact,
A\simeq A^{s}
is the final object of the diagram and all morphisms belong to
W_{q}
.

We fix
A
in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
and
consider the functor

H_{A}:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\to\mathbf{CGroups}(\mathbf{Spc})\ ,\quad B\mapsto\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}((q^{n}A)^{s},B)\ ,

(11.13)

i.e., we insert the diagram (
11.12
) into the first argument of the mapping space and take the colimit.
The following result is a version of
[
Cun87
, Prop. 2.1]
.

Proposition 11.7
.

The functor
H_{A}
is split-exact.

Proof.

We consider a split-exact sequence

0\to I\xrightarrow{j}B\xrightarrow{\pi}Q\to 0

(11.14)

of
C^{*}
-algebras
with split
s:Q\to B
. We must show that

H_{A}(I^{s})\oplus H_{A}(Q^{s})\xrightarrow{H_{A}(j^{s})+H_{A}(s^{s})}H_{A}(B^{s})

is an equivalence, where the superscript
s
stands for
K
-stabilization as in (
11.6
).

We first observe that it suffices to show that this map induces an isomorphism of groups of connected components.
For
i
in
{\rm Nature}
we write
H_{A,i}(-):=\pi_{i}H_{A}(-)
for the corresponding abelian group valued functor. For every
i
in
{\rm Nature}
we have
a canonical isomorphism
H_{A,i}(-)\cong H_{A,0}(S^{i}(-))
, where
S^{i}(-):=C_{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{i})\otimes-
is the
i
-fold suspension functor. Using the fact that the functor

S^{i}(-)
preserves exact sequences we see that
it suffices to show
that

H_{A,0}(I^{s})\oplus H_{A,0}(Q^{s})\xrightarrow{H_{A,0}(j^{s})\oplus H_{A,0}(s^{s})}H_{A,0}(B^{s})

(11.15)

is an isomorphism for all split-exact sequences (
11.14
).

We now use that
L_{K}
is a left Bousfield localization
and
3.8
, or directly (
4.3
),
in order see that

{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}((q^{n}A)^{s},(-)^{s})\simeq{\mathtt{Map}}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(q^{n}A,(-)^{s})\simeq\ell\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(q^{n}A,(-)^{s})

(11.16)

as a functors from
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
to
\mathbf{Spc}
. Combining this with (
11.13
)
we get an equivalence

H_{A}(B)\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}\left(\ell\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(A,B^{s})\xrightarrow{\iota_{A}^{*}}\ell\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(qA,B^{s})\xrightarrow{\iota_{qA}^{*}}\ell\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(q^{2}A,B^{s})\xrightarrow{\iota_{q^{2}A}^{*}}\dots\right)\ .

We define a map

l_{n}:\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(q^{n}A,B^{s})\to\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(q^{n+1}A,I^{s})

as follows.
Let
f:q^{n}A\to B^{s}
be an element of
\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(q^{n}A,B^{s})
. Then we get an associated map

(f,s^{s}\pi^{s}f):q^{n}A*q^{n}A\to B^{s}
with the components as indicated, see
11.2
. We observe that

\pi^{s}\circ(f,s^{s}\pi^{s}f)\circ i_{q^{n}A}=0
, where
i_{q^{n}A}:q^{n+1}A\to q^{n}A*q^{n}A
is the canonical inclusion. Therefore we can define

l_{n}(f):=(f,s^{s}\pi^{s}f)\circ i_{q^{n}A}:q^{n+1}A\to I^{s}\ .

For
g:q^{n}A\to I^{s}
we have

l_{n}(j^{s}\circ g)=g\circ\iota_{q^{n}A}
.
Finally, for
h:q^{n}A\to Q^{s}
we have

l_{n}(s^{s}\circ h)=(s^{s}\circ h,s^{s}\circ h)\circ i_{q^{n}A}=0
.

We next show that
H_{A,0}(j^{s})
is injective.
Let
g:q^{n}A\to I^{s}
represent an element
[g]
in
H_{A,0}(I^{s})
such that
H_{A,0}(j^{s})([g])=0
.
Then there exists
m
in
{\rm Nature}
with
n\leq m
such that

[j^{s}\circ g\circ\iota_{q^{n}A}\circ\dots\iota_{q^{m-1}A}]=0
in
\pi_{0}\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(q^{m}A,B^{s})\ .
But then

0=[l_{m}(j^{s}\circ g\circ\iota_{q^{n}A}\circ\dots\iota_{q^{m-1}A})]=[g\circ\iota_{q^{n}A}\circ\dots\iota_{q^{m-1}A}\circ\iota_{q^{m}A}]

in
\pi_{0}\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(q^{m+1}A,I^{s})
. This implies that
[g]=0
.

We now show that the family of maps
(l_{n})_{n}
determines a well-defined map

l:H_{A,0}(B^{s})\to H_{A,0}(I^{s})
. Let
[f]
in
H_{A,0}(B^{s})
be represented by a map

f:q^{n}A\to B^{s}
. Then

H_{A,0}(j^{s})([l_{n}(f)])=[f]-H_{A,0}(s^{s}\circ\pi^{s})([f])\ .

The right-hand side does not depend on the choice of the representative. By the injectivity of statement above we conclude that

[l_{n}(f)]
is well-defined.

We thus have

H_{A,0}(j^{s})\circ l+H_{A,0}(s^{s})\circ H_{A,0}(\pi^{s})={\mathtt{id}}_{H_{A,0}}(B)\ ,

l\circ H_{A,0}(j^{s})={\mathtt{id}}_{H_{A,0}(I^{s})}\ ,\qquad l\circ H_{A,0}(s^{s})=0\ ,\qquad H_{A,0}(p^{s})\circ H_{A,0}(s^{s})={\mathtt{id}}_{H_{A,0}(Q^{s})}\ .

These equalities imply that (
11.15
) is an isomorphism.
∎

The putative right calculus of fractions is called a
right calculus of fractions
in the sense of
[
Cis19
, Def. 7.2.6]
if
the functor
H_{A}(-)
from (
11.13
) sends the morphisms from
W_{q}
to equivalences.

Proposition 11.8
.

1.

W(A)
is a right-calculus of fractions.

2.

We have

H_{A}(B)\simeq{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,q}}(A,B)\ .

(11.17)

3.

The
\infty
-category
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,q}
is additive.

4.

The localization
L_{q}
is left-exact.

5.

We have an essentially unique commutative diagram

81.37514pt\raise-57.3861pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{L}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 143.24852pt\raise-62.77777pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 86.38255pt\raise-62.77777pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,q}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ ,

(11.18)

where
L
is a left exact functor.

Proof.

The Assertion
1
follows from

11.7
and
11.4
.

The Assertion
2
follows from
1
and
the general formula
[
Cis19
, Def. 7.2.8]
for the mapping spaces in a localization in the presence of a right calculus of fractions.

For Assertion
3
note that by (
11.17
) the mapping spaces in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,q}
are commutative groups.

Assertion
4
is a consequence of the formula (combine (
11.13
) and (
11.17
))

{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,q}}(A,B)\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\rm Nature}}{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}((q^{n}A)^{s},B)

(11.19)

for the mapping space as a colimit over the filtered poset
{\rm Nature}
and the fact that filtered colimits in
\mathbf{Spc}
commute with finite limits.

We finally show Assertion
5
. The two upper triangles in (
11.18
) reflect the definitions of the maps.
By Assertion
2
the functor
H_{A}(-)
represents the mapping space in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,q}
. Since it is split-exact by
11.7

the localization
L_{q}
sends (the images under
L_{h,K}
of) split-exact sequences
to fibre sequences. The composition
L_{h,K,q}
is thus homotopy invariant, stable, and Schochet- and split-exact. By the universal property of
L_{h,K,\mathrm{splt}}
stated in
5.7
.
2
we get the map
L
and the two-morphism filling of the outer triangle
in (
11.18
). We finally use
the universal property (
4.4
) of
L_{h,K}
in order to define the two-morphism filling the lower triangle.
∎

Proposition 11.9
.

The functor
L
from (
11.18
) is equivalent to the right adjoint of a right Bousfield localization

\mathrm{incl}:L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,\mathrm{splt}}}^{\mathrm{group}}\leftrightarrows L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,\mathrm{splt}}:R\ .

Proof.

We call a functor on
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
split-exact if it sends the elements of
W_{\mathrm{splt}}
to equivalences.
We first show that
L_{\mathrm{splt}}\circ q^{s}
is split-exact and left-exact
and therefore by
5.7
.
1
descends to a left-exact functor

R:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,\mathrm{splt}}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,\mathrm{splt}}\ .

The functor
L_{\mathrm{splt}}:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,\mathrm{splt}}
is split-exact by definition, and it is left exact by

5.7
.
1
.
The functor

L_{\mathrm{splt}}\circ q^{s}
sends
A
in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
to the fibre of

L_{\mathrm{splt}}((A*A)^{s})\xrightarrow{d_{A}^{s}}L_{\mathrm{splt}}(A^{s})
, where
d_{A}:A*A\to A
is the fold map.
Since
L_{\mathrm{splt}}((A*A)^{s})\simeq L_{\mathrm{splt}}(A^{s})\times L_{\mathrm{splt}}(A^{s})
by semi-additivity
we can identify
L_{\mathrm{splt}}\circ q^{s}
with the functor which sends
A
to the fibre of some natural map

L_{\mathrm{splt}}(A^{s})\times L_{\mathrm{splt}}(A^{s})\to L_{\mathrm{splt}}(A^{s})
between split-exact and left exact functors.
We conclude that
L_{\mathrm{splt}}\circ q^{s}
itself is split-exact and left exact.

As a consequence of
11.3
and the fact that
L_{\mathrm{splt}}
preserves products the functor
R
takes values in
L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,\mathrm{splt}}}^{\mathrm{group}}
. We have a natural transformation

\kappa:=L_{\mathrm{splt}}(\iota^{s}):R\to{\mathtt{id}}_{L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,\mathrm{splt}}}}:L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,\mathrm{splt}}}\to L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,\mathrm{splt}}}\ .

(11.20)

If
A
in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
has the property that
L_{\mathrm{splt}}(A)
is a group, then
\kappa_{L_{\mathrm{splt}}(A)}\simeq L_{\mathrm{splt}}(\iota^{s}_{A})
is an equivalence by
11.4

since

L_{\mathrm{splt}}
is split-exact.
Since
L_{\mathrm{splt}}
, being a Dwyer-Kan localization, is essentially surjective
we can conclude that the essential image of
R
is
L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,\mathrm{splt}}}^{\mathrm{group}}
.

In particular we see that
\kappa_{R(A)}
is an equivalence for very
A
in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,\mathrm{splt}}
.
Since

R\circ L_{\mathrm{splt}}\simeq L_{\mathrm{splt}}\circ q^{s}
is split-exact and takes values in groups we can conclude again by
11.4
that

R(\kappa_{A})
is also an equivalence for every
A
.

As explained at the beginning of
Section

7
this implies that
R

is the right-adjoint of a right Bousfield localization

\mathrm{incl}:L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,\mathrm{splt}}}^{\mathrm{group}}\leftrightarrows L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,\mathrm{splt}}:R

(11.21)

with counit
\kappa
.

The functor
L
inverts the morphisms
\kappa_{A}
since

L(\kappa_{A})\simeq L_{q}(\iota^{s}_{A})
. This gives the factorization
L^{\prime}
in the diagram

\textstyle{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{L_{q}}
\scriptstyle{L_{\mathrm{splt}}}
\textstyle{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,\mathrm{splt}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{L}
\scriptstyle{R}
\textstyle{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,q}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{L^{\prime,-1}}
\textstyle{L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,\mathrm{splt}}}^{\mathrm{group}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{L^{\prime}}

Since
R\circ L_{\mathrm{splt}}
sends the morphisms
\iota^{s}_{A}
to the equivalences
R(\kappa_{A})
for all
A
in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}

we get an inverse to
L^{\prime}

from the universal property of
L_{q}
.
∎

Recall the definition (
11.11
) of
L_{h,K,q}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,q}
.

Proposition 11.10
.

For any left-exact and additive
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
we have an equivalence

L_{h,K,q}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,q},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s,splt+Sch}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

(11.22)

Proof.

This follows from the following chain of equivalences

\displaystyle{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,q},{\mathbf{D}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L^{\prime,*}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}(L_{K}{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,\mathrm{splt}}}^{\mathrm{group}},{\mathbf{D}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle R^{*}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,\mathrm{splt}},{\mathbf{D}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L_{h,K,\mathrm{splt}}^{*},\eqref{ewfwqedqewdedqwd}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,s,splt+Sch}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

In order to see that
R^{*}
is an equivalence note that
the components
\kappa_{A}:R(A)\to A
of the natural transformation (
11.20
) generate the Dwyer-Kan localization

R
. As a
consequence of
11.4
any left exact functor

L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,\mathrm{splt}}\to{\mathbf{D}}
to an additive
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
sends theses components to equivalences.
∎

Proposition 11.11
.

For
?
in
\{\min,\max\}

the localization
L_{q}
has a symmetric monoidal refinement and the tensor product
\otimes_{?}
on

L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,q}
is bi-left exact.

Proof.

Since
L_{\mathrm{splt}}
has a symmetric monoidal refinement with a bi-left exact tensor product by
7.2
it suffices to show that the functor
L
has one.
As seen in the proof of
11.9

we have a functor which sends
A
in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,\mathrm{splt}}
to the diagram

We can conclude that

\kappa_{A\otimes B}\simeq\kappa_{A}\otimes B
.
In particular,
-\otimes B
preserves the generators of the Dwyer-Kan localization
L
which therefore has a symmetric monoidal refinement. Furthermore,
-\otimes B
descends to a left-exact functor on
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,q}
.
∎

For every symmetric monoidal additive
\infty
-category we thus get an equivalence

L_{h,K,q}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{\mathrm{lex}}(L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,q},{\mathbf{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{h,s,splt+Sch}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{D}})\ .

(11.23)

Remark 11.12
.

In this remark we provide the bridge to Kasparov modules. We refer to
[
Kas88
]
,
[
Bla98
]

for a detailed theory.
Let
f:qA\to B\otimes K
be a homomorphism with components
\hat{f}_{i}:A\to M(B\otimes K)
. The corresponding

(A,B)
-bimodule
(H,\phi,F)
is the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded
B
-Hilbert
C^{*}
-module
L^{2}(B)\oplus L^{2}(B)

with the odd endomorphism

F:=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&1\\
1&0\end{array}\right)

and

\phi:=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\hat{f}_{0}&0\\
0&\hat{f}_{1}\end{array}\right):A\to{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(B(L^{2}(B))\ ,

(11.24)

where in order to interpret (
11.24
) we identify
B(L^{2}(B))\cong M(B\otimes K)
in the canonical way.
For separable
C^{*}
-algebras
A,B
we
interpret

\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(qA,K\otimes B)
as the topological space of
(A,B)
-Kasparov modules in the Cuntz picture.
Its underlying space

\ell\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(qA,K\otimes B)\simeq{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}}(qA,B)\simeq{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}}(q^{s}A,B)

(11.25)

has a natural refinement to a commutative monoid in spaces. This monoid structure reflects the direct sum of Kasparov modules. Since groups and cogroups in a semi-additive
\infty
-category coincide

11.3
implies that
\ell\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(qA,K\otimes B)
it is actually a commutative group.
We have a natural map of commutative groups

\ell\underline{{\mathtt{Hom}}}(qA,K\otimes B)\xrightarrow{}{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,q}}(A,B)\ ,

(11.26)

given by the composition
of (
11.25
)
with the canonical map from the right-hand side of this equivalence to the second stage of the colimit in (
11.19
).
By
11.14
below we see that this map is actually an equivalence presenting the commutative mapping groups in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,q}
in terms of spaces of Kasparov modules.

∎

For completeness of the presentation we now discuss
[
Cun87
, Thm. 1.6]
. All of the above has a version for separable algebras which we will indicate by an additional subscript
\mathrm{sep}
.
Let
A
be a separable
C^{*}
-algebra.

Theorem 11.13

(
[
Cun87
, Thm. 1.6]
)
.

There exists a homomorphism
\phi:qA\to{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(q^{2}A)
such that
{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(\iota_{qA})\circ\phi:qA\to{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(qA)

and
\phi\circ\iota_{qA}:q^{2}A\to{\mathtt{Mat}}_{2}(q^{2}A)
are homotopic to the left upper corner inclusions.

Before we sketch the proof we derive the consequences of
Theorem

11.13
.

Corollary 11.14
.

For separable
C^{*}
-algebras
A
and
B
the morphism (
11.26
) is an equivalence.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of (
11.19
),
(
11.16
) and
Theorem

11.13
which implies that the colimit in
(
11.19
) stabilizes from
n=1
on.
∎

Proposition 11.15
.

The functor
q_{\mathrm{sep}}^{s}:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\to q_{\mathrm{sep}}^{s}L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
is the right-adjoint of a Bousfield localization

\mathrm{incl}:q_{\mathrm{sep}}^{s}L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\leftrightarrows L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}:q_{\mathrm{sep}}^{s}

and

q_{\mathrm{sep}}^{s}:L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\to q_{\mathrm{sep}}^{s}L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}

represents its target as the Dwyer-Kan localization
at the set
\hat{W}_{\mathrm{sep},q}
from the separable version of (
11.9
).

Proof.

Let
\mathrm{incl}:q_{\mathrm{sep}}^{s}L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\to L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
denote the inclusion of the full subcategory of
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
on the image of
q_{\mathrm{sep}}^{s}
. We have a natural transformation

\iota^{s}:\mathrm{incl}\circ q_{\mathrm{sep}}^{s}\to{\mathtt{id}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}}\ .

(11.27)

For
A
in
q_{\mathrm{sep}}^{s}L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
and
B
be in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
the binatural
transformation

\hskip-11.38092pt{\mathtt{Map}}_{q^{s}_{\mathrm{sep}}L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}}(A,q^{s}_{\mathrm{sep}}B)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{incl}}{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}}(\mathrm{incl}A,\mathrm{incl}(q^{s}_{\mathrm{sep}}B))\xrightarrow{\iota^{s}_{B,*}}{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}}(\mathrm{incl}A,B)

is an equivalence. To this end we set

A=q_{\mathrm{sep}}^{s}A^{\prime}
for some
A^{\prime}
in
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
and factorize the map as a composition of equivalences

\displaystyle{\mathtt{Map}}_{q^{s}_{\mathrm{sep}}L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}}(A,q^{s}_{\mathrm{sep}}B)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{incl}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}}(q_{\mathrm{sep}}^{s}A^{\prime},q_{\mathrm{sep}}^{s}B)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{wejogiwerfgerwfrewfwerfw}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,q}}(A^{\prime},q_{\mathrm{sep}}^{s}B)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota^{s}_{B,*}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,q}}(A^{\prime},B)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{wejogiwerfgerwfrewfwerfw}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle{\mathtt{Map}}_{L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}}(\mathrm{incl}A,B)

We conclude that (
11.27
) is the counit of a right Bousfield localization.
Since the right Bousfield localization is a Dwyer Kan localization at the set of the components of its counit
we conclude the second assertion by a comparison with (
11.9
).
∎

Recall the construction (
4.8

C^{*}
-algebras.

Corollary 11.16
.

The category
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,q}
admits countable coproducts which are represented by the free product and also by
the sum in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

Proof.

Since
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,q}
is a right Bousfield localization of
L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
it inhertits all colimits from the latter category and the inclusion functor, being a left adjoint, preserves them. The assertion now follows from
4.11
.
4
.
∎
